This ability furthermore extends beyond standard language tasks, enabling few-shot learning for general patterns. In this work, we consider the application of in-context learning with pre-trained language models for dynamic manipulation. Dynamic manipulation introduces several crucial challenges, including increased dimensionality, complex dynamics, and partial observability. To address this, we take an iterative approach, and formulate our in-context learning problem to predict adjustments to a parametric policy based on previous interactions. We show across several tasks in simulation and on a physical robot that utilizing in-context learning outperforms alternative methods in the low data regime. Video summary of this work and experiments can be found https://youtu.be/2inxpdrq74U?si=dAdDYsUEr25nZvRn.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a method called In-Context Policy Improvement (ICPI) that leverages pre-trained Large Language Models (LLMs) for sample-efficient dynamic manipulation policy improvement. The approach utilizes in-context learning, where the LLM is provided with a few examples of policy adjustments based on previous interactions with a dynamic system. The LLM then learns to predict adjustments to a parametric policy based on the current policy parameters and state trajectory. The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of ICPI on various dynamic manipulation tasks in simulation and on a physical robot, showing that it outperforms alternative methods in the low-data regime.  The key idea is to treat policy improvement as a sequence-to-sequence problem, allowing the LLM to act as a general pattern machine for learning the improvement operator.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The application of in-context learning with LLMs to dynamic manipulation is relatively novel.  While previous works have explored LLMs for high-level planning and code generation in robotics, this paper specifically tackles the challenges of dynamic manipulation, where direct textual or visual representations are often insufficient. The formulation of policy improvement as a sequence-to-sequence completion task for LLMs is also a novel contribution.

* **Significance:** The significance of the paper lies in its potential to enable robots to learn and adapt dynamic manipulation skills more efficiently. Dynamic manipulation is inherently difficult to model and control, often requiring extensive data collection and task-specific training. The ICPI method reduces the data requirements by leveraging the pre-trained knowledge of LLMs and enabling few-shot learning. This can have a significant impact on real-world robotics applications, where data collection can be expensive and time-consuming.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Sample Efficiency:** The paper demonstrates significant sample efficiency compared to traditional methods like reinforcement learning or Bayesian optimization.
    * **Generalization:** The in-context learning approach allows for generalization to new task instances and environments with minimal data.
    * **Real-World Experiments:** The inclusion of experiments on a physical robot strengthens the validity and practical relevance of the method.
    * **Clear Formulation:** The formulation of policy improvement as a sequence-to-sequence task is well-defined and easy to understand.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on Parametric Policies:** The method relies on a pre-defined parametric policy, which may limit the flexibility and expressiveness of the learned skills. This limits the scope of the approach since finding a 'good' parametric policy may be difficult.
    * **Limited Scope of Dynamic Manipulation Tasks:** While the paper demonstrates the method on several dynamic manipulation tasks, the tasks are still relatively simple compared to the full complexity of real-world dynamic manipulation.
    * **Computational Cost:** Using large pre-trained language models is computationally expensive, which is a practical limitation.
    * **Lack of Comparative Analysis with State-of-the-Art Dynamic Manipulation Methods:** The paper compares the proposed method to relatively basic baselines. A comparison to a state-of-the-art dynamic manipulation technique (even if requiring more data) would strengthen the claims.
    * **Dependence on the Quality of the "Guiding" Executions:** The approach relies on a brute-force method for constructing its training data. This method is only suitable in narrow situations. The choice of the parameters to the training data generation method may substantially affect the downstream results.

* **Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by demonstrating the power of LLMs for dynamic manipulation tasks. It provides a new perspective on how LLMs can be used to solve complex robotic control problems beyond high-level planning. The ICPI method can be further developed and extended to more complex tasks and environments.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of robotic manipulation by demonstrating the application of in-context learning with LLMs to dynamic manipulation tasks. The method achieves sample efficiency and good generalization performance.  However, there are limitations related to computational cost, the reliance on predefined parametric policies, and a relatively simple dynamic manipulation task. Most importantly, the construction of the training data relies on a relatively unsophisticated method of search. While the paper shows that in-context learning can work on robotic control tasks, the method is likely not ready for 'real world' manipulation scenarios, and it's unclear if these sorts of scenarios can even be realized with this approach. Considering these factors, I would assign a score of 7.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### Reversible Unfolding Network for Concealed Visual Perception with Generative Refinement
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15027v1)
- **Authors**: Chunming He, Fengyang Xiao, Rihan Zhang, Chengyu Fang, Deng-Ping Fan, Sina Farsiu
- **Abstract**: Existing methods for concealed visual perception (CVP) often leverage reversible strategies to decrease uncertainty, yet these are typically confined to the mask domain, leaving the potential of the RGB domain underexplored. To address this, we propose a reversible unfolding network with generative refinement, termed RUN++. Specifically, RUN++ first formulates the CVP task as a mathematical optimization problem and unfolds the iterative solution into a multi-stage deep network. This approach provides a principled way to apply reversible modeling across both mask and RGB domains while leveraging a diffusion model to resolve the resulting uncertainty. Each stage of the network integrates three purpose-driven modules: a Concealed Object Region Extraction (CORE) module applies reversible modeling to the mask domain to identify core object regions; a Context-Aware Region Enhancement (CARE) module extends this principle to the RGB domain to foster better foreground-background separation; and a Finetuning Iteration via Noise-based Enhancement (FINE) module provides a final refinement. The FINE module introduces a targeted Bernoulli diffusion model that refines only the uncertain regions of the segmentation mask, harnessing the generative power of diffusion for fine-detail restoration without the prohibitive computational cost of a full-image process. This unique synergy, where the unfolding network provides a strong uncertainty prior for the diffusion model, allows RUN++ to efficiently direct its focus toward ambiguous areas, significantly mitigating false positives and negatives. Furthermore, we introduce a new paradigm for building robust CVP systems that remain effective under real-world degradations and extend this concept into a broader bi-level optimization framework.
- **Summary**: The paper presents RUN++, a novel deep unfolding network for concealed visual perception (CVP). It addresses the limitations of existing CVP methods by formulating the task as a mathematical optimization problem and unfolding its iterative solution into a multi-stage deep network. This allows reversible modeling across both the mask and RGB domains. A Bernoulli diffusion model is integrated to refine uncertain regions of the segmentation mask, enhancing fine-detail restoration. The paper also introduces a paradigm for building robust CVP systems that are effective under real-world degradations, extending the concept into a bi-level optimization framework. Extensive experiments across various CVP tasks demonstrate state-of-the-art performance and flexibility.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces several novel components. The combination of a deep unfolding network with a diffusion model for CVP is a fresh approach. The CORE and CARE modules contribute to reversible modeling of foreground and background.  The FINE module with a targeted Bernoulli diffusion model refiner represents an innovative way to leverage generative models for fine-grained detail restoration. The bi-level collaborative optimization framework also adds to the novelty. The extension from RUN to RUN++ is significant.

*   **Significance:** CVP is a challenging problem with broad applications. The proposed method achieves state-of-the-art results across multiple datasets and tasks, indicating a significant advance in the field.  The efficiency gains reported in the paper as well as the generalization to multiple tasks is significant. The new approach might change the architecture of future CVP models.

*   **Strengths:** The method is well-motivated and addresses a key limitation of existing approaches. The mathematical formulation provides a solid foundation.  The experimental results are comprehensive and convincingly demonstrate the superiority of the proposed method. The ablation studies are thorough and provide useful insights. The code will be publicly released.
*   **Weaknesses:** The model is computationally intensive, despite attempts to address efficiency. The individual contribution of every component is not obvious, in particular the Bernoulli diffusion model in an unfolding network, and more analysis could help in better understanding each component's role. Even if the model is based on an unfolding strategy, there may be more simplification to reduce computations.

*   **Impact:** The paper is likely to have a significant impact on the CVP field. The results are strong, and it should provide a solid benchmark for future research. Also, the discussion on the advantage of Deep Unfolding Networks for concealed visual perception adds value, and the proposed direction of bi-level optimization framework has a great potential for improving low-light object detection.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper demonstrates significant advancements in CVP, combining deep unfolding networks with diffusion models in a novel and effective manner. The comprehensive experimental validation, thorough ablation studies, and strong performance gains across multiple datasets and tasks demonstrate its contribution to the field. It also makes a strong case for adopting and expanding upon the benefits of Deep Unfolding Networks as a backbone for solving many computer vision tasks. There are only limited weaknesses relating to complexity, but that may motivate future work.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### MoEcho: Exploiting Side-Channel Attacks to Compromise User Privacy in Mixture-of-Experts LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15036v1)
- **Authors**: Ruyi Ding, Tianhong Xu, Xinyi Shen, Aidong Adam Ding, Yunsi Fei
- **Abstract**: The transformer architecture has become a cornerstone of modern AI, fueling remarkable progress across applications in natural language processing, computer vision, and multimodal learning. As these models continue to scale explosively for performance, implementation efficiency remains a critical challenge. Mixture of Experts (MoE) architectures, selectively activating specialized subnetworks (experts), offer a unique balance between model accuracy and computational cost. However, the adaptive routing in MoE architectures, where input tokens are dynamically directed to specialized experts based on their semantic meaning inadvertently opens up a new attack surface for privacy breaches. These input-dependent activation patterns leave distinctive temporal and spatial traces in hardware execution, which adversaries could exploit to deduce sensitive user data. In this work, we propose MoEcho, discovering a side channel analysis based attack surface that compromises user privacy on MoE based systems. Specifically, in MoEcho, we introduce four novel architectural side channels on different computing platforms, including Cache Occupancy Channels and Pageout+Reload on CPUs, and Performance Counter and TLB Evict+Reload on GPUs, respectively. Exploiting these vulnerabilities, we propose four attacks that effectively breach user privacy in large language models (LLMs) and vision language models (VLMs) based on MoE architectures: Prompt Inference Attack, Response Reconstruction Attack, Visual Inference Attack, and Visual Reconstruction Attack. MoEcho is the first runtime architecture level security analysis of the popular MoE structure common in modern transformers, highlighting a serious security and privacy threat and calling for effective and timely safeguards when harnessing MoE based models for developing efficient large scale AI services.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "MoEcho: Exploiting Side-Channel Attacks to Compromise User Privacy in Mixture-of-Experts LLMs":

**Summary:**

The paper "MoEcho" introduces a novel attack surface on Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) Large Language Models (LLMs) and Vision Language Models (VLMs) that leverages side-channel analysis to compromise user privacy. The attack exploits the dynamic expert activation patterns in MoE architectures, which leave distinct temporal and spatial traces in hardware execution. MoEcho proposes four novel architectural side-channels on CPU and GPU platforms: Cache Occupancy Channels, Pageout+Reload (CPUs), Performance Counter, and TLB Evict+Reload (GPUs). These side-channels are then utilized to develop four privacy attacks: Prompt Inference Attack, Response Reconstruction Attack, Visual Attribute Inference Attack, and Visual Reconstruction Attack.  The authors evaluate their attacks on various MoE-based models (including DeepSeek), demonstrating significant success in inferring sensitive information from user inputs and reconstructing model outputs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel attack surface by exploiting the input-dependent execution patterns inherent in MoE architectures. While side-channel attacks on DNNs are not new, their application to MoE models and the specific architectural side-channels identified (Cache Occupancy, Pageout+Reload, Performance Counters, and TLB Evict+Reload) appear to be novel. The creation of specific attacks for LLMs and VLMs based on this understanding also adds to the paper's original contribution. This is the first architecture-level security analysis of the popular MoE structure.

*   **Significance:** The work highlights a serious security and privacy threat in a widely adopted architecture for efficient large-scale AI services. MoE models are increasingly prevalent, and the demonstrated vulnerability could have broad implications. The paper motivates the urgent need for effective safeguards to protect user privacy when deploying MoE-based models.

*   **Technical Depth & Soundness:** The technical approach seems well-reasoned and executed. The design of the side-channels is thorough, and the attack methodologies are clearly explained. The use of real-world datasets and open-source models adds to the credibility of the evaluation. The paper appears to follow rigorous security assessment methodologies with clear threat models, and attack modeling and implementation details were provided.

*   **Scope & Generalizability:** The attacks are primarily demonstrated on the DeepSeek architecture, although the authors argue that similar MoE designs are also susceptible. While the core vulnerability stems from the MoE architecture itself, some specifics may be implementation-dependent. A broader evaluation on other popular MoE architectures (Mixtral, Llama 4, Grok, etc.) would further strengthen the generalizability of the findings.

*   **Mitigation Strategies:** The paper discusses potential mitigation strategies but does not provide concrete implementations or evaluations of these defenses. Future work focused on developing and validating effective defenses would be a valuable addition.

*   **Ethical Concerns:** The authors addressed ethical considerations. They disclosed the vulnerability to the open-source community (Hugging Face) and the hardware manufacturers (NVIDIA). The use of open-source models and controlled laboratory environments helps mitigate potential misuse of the attack techniques.

**Strengths:**

*   Novel attack surface identified and clearly explained.
*   Well-defined threat model and attack methodologies.
*   Rigorous experimental evaluation with open-source models and real-world datasets.
*   Highlights the urgent need for privacy safeguards in MoE-based systems.

**Weaknesses:**

*   Evaluation is primarily focused on the DeepSeek architecture. Broader evaluation on other popular MoE architectures would strengthen the findings.
*   Mitigation strategies are discussed but not implemented or evaluated.
*   Some of the side-channels are tied to specific hardware features which are subject to change.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:** The paper makes a significant contribution by identifying a novel and practically relevant attack surface on a widely adopted architecture in large-scale AI. The technical approach is sound, and the experimental results demonstrate the seriousness of the privacy threat. The paper is well-written, clearly presents its findings, and provides a reasonable discussion of potential mitigation strategies. Although the evaluation is primarily focused on one architecture (DeepSeek) and defense strategies are not validated, the novelty and significance of the identified vulnerability warrant a high score.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 8/10

### Reward-Shifted Speculative Sampling Is An Efficient Test-Time Weak-to-Strong Aligner
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15044v1)
- **Authors**: Bolian Li, Yanran Wu, Xinyu Luo, Ruqi Zhang
- **Abstract**: Aligning large language models (LLMs) with human preferences has become a critical step in their development. Recent research has increasingly focused on test-time alignment, where additional compute is allocated during inference to enhance LLM safety and reasoning capabilities. However, these test-time alignment techniques often incur substantial inference costs, limiting their practical application. We are inspired by the speculative sampling acceleration, which leverages a small draft model to efficiently predict future tokens, to address the efficiency bottleneck of test-time alignment. We introduce the reward-Shifted Speculative Sampling (SSS) algorithm, in which the draft model is aligned with human preferences, while the target model remains unchanged. We theoretically demonstrate that the distributional shift between the aligned draft model and the unaligned target model can be exploited to recover the RLHF optimal solution without actually obtaining it, by modifying the acceptance criterion and bonus token distribution. Our algorithm achieves superior gold reward scores at a significantly reduced inference cost in test-time weak-to-strong alignment experiments, thereby validating both its effectiveness and efficiency.
- **Summary**: The paper introduces Reward-Shifted Speculative Sampling (SSS), a novel test-time alignment algorithm that combines speculative sampling with aligning a small "draft" model to human preferences. Unlike many existing test-time alignment methods that rely on an external reward model and frequent LLM calls, SSS aligns a computationally cheaper draft model and then uses a (potentially unaligned) target model for verification. The paper theoretically shows that SSS can recover the RLHF optimal solution and empirically demonstrates its efficiency and effectiveness in test-time weak-to-strong alignment tasks, achieving higher gold reward scores at significantly reduced inference costs compared to existing baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the combined approach of using a reward-shifted draft model within a speculative sampling framework *without* relying on an external reward model during inference. This is a departure from typical test-time alignment strategies, which usually involve interacting with a separate reward model throughout the decoding process. The redefinition of the speculative sampling objective from recovering the target model's distribution to recovering the RLHF optimal solution is also a noteworthy contribution. While speculative sampling has been used before, its application specifically for test-time alignment in this configuration is relatively novel.

**Significance:** The significance comes from the potential to reduce the computational cost associated with test-time alignment. The ability to achieve better alignment (higher reward) *and* improved efficiency (lower latency and computational cost) makes SSS a potentially valuable contribution to the field, enabling more practical deployment of aligned LLMs in resource-constrained environments.  The theoretical justification also strengthens the contribution.

**Strengths:**

*   **Efficiency:** The core strength is the demonstrated efficiency, consistently outperforming baselines in terms of inference speedup and reduced LLM calls. This is crucial for practical deployment.
*   **Theoretical Justification:** The paper provides a theoretical basis for why SSS can recover the RLHF optimal solution, which adds credibility to the empirical results.
*   **No External Reward Model:** Removing the need for an external reward model significantly reduces complexity and latency.
*   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is generally well-written and explains the algorithm and experiments clearly.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Dependency on Draft Model Quality:** The performance of SSS is heavily dependent on the quality and alignment of the draft model. The paper acknowledges this limitation, stating that the "effectiveness of our algorithm depends on the assumption that the shifted draft model is well-aligned." Imperfect alignment of the draft model could potentially limit the effectiveness of SSS.
*   **Generalization of Draft Model Alignment:** The use of a small draft model to capture human preferences may suffer from generalization issues; the paper states that "a draft model aligned for one task may not perform well on others."
*   **Tuning Hyperparameters:** The authors also point out the challenges in tuning hyperparameters due to the lack of access to the reward model used for alignment.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a novel and promising approach to test-time alignment that addresses the crucial issue of computational efficiency. While the algorithm's performance is dependent on the quality of the draft model and may suffer from generalization issues, the potential for significant reductions in inference cost, coupled with a sound theoretical foundation, makes it a valuable contribution to the field. The dependence on the assumption of a well-aligned shifted draft model is also a significant limiting factor. The ablation studies and practical suggestions provided are also helpful.

Score: 7

The score of 7 reflects the novelty, significance, and potential impact of the SSS algorithm, while also accounting for its limitations and the critical dependence on draft model quality. Although there is a strong demonstration of performance and efficiency, the limitations prevent a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Emergent Crowds Dynamics from Language-Driven Multi-Agent Interactions
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15047v1)
- **Authors**: Yibo Liu, Liam Shatzel, Brandon Haworth, Teseo Schneider
- **Abstract**: Animating and simulating crowds using an agent-based approach is a well-established area where every agent in the crowd is individually controlled such that global human-like behaviour emerges. We observe that human navigation and movement in crowds are often influenced by complex social and environmental interactions, driven mainly by language and dialogue. However, most existing work does not consider these dimensions and leads to animations where agent-agent and agent-environment interactions are largely limited to steering and fixed higher-level goal extrapolation. We propose a novel method that exploits large language models (LLMs) to control agents' movement. Our method has two main components: a dialogue system and language-driven navigation. We periodically query agent-centric LLMs conditioned on character personalities, roles, desires, and relationships to control the generation of inter-agent dialogue when necessitated by the spatial and social relationships with neighbouring agents. We then use the conversation and each agent's personality, emotional state, vision, and physical state to control the navigation and steering of each agent. Our model thus enables agents to make motion decisions based on both their perceptual inputs and the ongoing dialogue. We validate our method in two complex scenarios that exemplify the interplay between social interactions, steering, and crowding. In these scenarios, we observe that grouping and ungrouping of agents automatically occur. Additionally, our experiments show that our method serves as an information-passing mechanism within the crowd. As a result, our framework produces more realistic crowd simulations, with emergent group behaviours arising naturally from any environmental setting.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel method for simulating crowd dynamics by integrating large language models (LLMs) into a decentralized agent-based framework.  Unlike traditional crowd simulations that rely on steering and fixed goals, this approach enables agents to interact through language, influencing each other's behavior and the overall crowd dynamics.  The system uses LLMs to generate dialogue based on agent personalities, relationships, and perceived environment.  This dialogue then influences agent navigation and steering decisions. The method is validated through simulations of a university club fair and a museum accident, demonstrating emergent behaviors like grouping and information propagation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The integration of LLMs for generating dialogue *and* using that dialogue to directly drive low-level movement and behavior in a decentralized agent simulation is a significant contribution. Existing work often focuses on LLMs for high-level planning or language interaction without tightly coupling it to physical motion in a nuanced way.  The use of personality traits and relationships to condition the LLM also adds depth. The method goes beyond simple steering and fixed goal extrapolation, which is a notable advancement.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a key limitation of traditional crowd simulations: the lack of realistic social interaction and its impact on movement. By introducing language-driven interactions, the authors create more believable and dynamic crowd behaviors. The observed emergent behaviors, such as grouping and information diffusion, highlight the potential of this approach for creating richer and more realistic simulations.  This has implications for architecture, animation, and social behavior analysis.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Decentralized Approach:** The decision-making is distributed among the agents, leading to more natural and less predictable behaviors.
    *   **Integration of Language and Motion:** The tight coupling of dialogue and movement creates a more holistic and realistic simulation.
    *   **Emergent Behaviors:** The simulations demonstrate the emergence of complex group behaviors without explicit programming, highlighting the potential for discovering unexpected patterns.
    *   **Relatability:** The examples are well thought out and showcase social interactions we often experience within crowds such as at a university club fair or museum.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational Cost:** Using LLMs for every agent decision is computationally expensive and limits the scalability of the simulation, as noted in the paper. The reliance on a remote LLM API also adds latency.
    *   **Lack of Quantitative Evaluation:** While the paper presents qualitative observations of emergent behaviors, it lacks quantitative metrics to objectively evaluate the realism and effectiveness of the simulation. For example, measuring the average group size, information diffusion rate, or deviation from predetermined paths could strengthen the analysis.
    *   **LLM Dependency & Validation:** As the authors have suggested, there are inherent issues in black-box LLM behavior validation. It is still necessary to validate whether the behaviors of the agents are realistic. More rigorous validation to study social emergent behaviour will be needed as the technique continues to evolve.
    *   **Over-reliance on LLMs:** While the LLM's role as the conductor of agent decisions contributes to the system's strengths in versatility and nuanced control, it simultaneously creates a weakness due to a dependence on the LLM's capacity to faithfully simulate real world emergent behaviour. If the LLM demonstrates inaccurate reasoning or deviates from typical behaviour patterns, it may lead to unrealistic simulation outcomes.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in crowd simulation, multi-agent systems, and social robotics. It demonstrates the power of LLMs for creating more realistic and interactive simulations. The work could also inspire new approaches to character animation and virtual world design.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel approach to crowd simulation with significant potential. The integration of LLMs to drive both dialogue and movement is a key contribution. However, the lack of quantitative evaluation, the computational cost, and the inherent challenges of validating LLM-driven behaviors limit its immediate impact. Therefore, a score of 7 is warranted. The paper offers a valuable proof-of-concept and lays the groundwork for future research, but requires further refinement and validation to fully realize its potential.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Don't Think Twice! Over-Reasoning Impairs Confidence Calibration
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15050v1)
- **Authors**: Romain Lacombe, Kerrie Wu, Eddie Dilworth
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models deployed as question answering tools require robust calibration to avoid overconfidence. We systematically evaluate how reasoning capabilities and budget affect confidence assessment accuracy, using the ClimateX dataset (Lacombe et al., 2023) and expanding it to human and planetary health. Our key finding challenges the "test-time scaling" paradigm: while recent reasoning LLMs achieve 48.7% accuracy in assessing expert confidence, increasing reasoning budgets consistently impairs rather than improves calibration. Extended reasoning leads to systematic overconfidence that worsens with longer thinking budgets, producing diminishing and negative returns beyond modest computational investments. Conversely, search-augmented generation dramatically outperforms pure reasoning, achieving 89.3% accuracy by retrieving relevant evidence. Our results suggest that information access, rather than reasoning depth or inference budget, may be the critical bottleneck for improved confidence calibration of knowledge-intensive tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the calibration of Large Language Models (LLMs) concerning human expert confidence in knowledge-intensive domains, specifically climate science (using the CLIMATEX dataset) and public health (IARC carcinogenicity dataset).  It challenges the "test-time scaling" paradigm, finding that increasing the reasoning budget (number of tokens allocated for deliberation) often *impairs* confidence calibration, leading to systematic overconfidence.  Search-augmented generation (RAG), however, significantly improves calibration accuracy by retrieving relevant external evidence.  The study suggests that information access, rather than reasoning depth, is a key bottleneck for accurate confidence assessment in these tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a reasonably novel finding: that simply increasing the reasoning budget of LLMs *worsens* confidence calibration in certain knowledge-intensive domains. This counterintuitive result is valuable, as it goes against the common assumption that more computation always leads to better performance. The expansion beyond climate to the health domain via the IARC dataset also adds to the novelty.

*   **Significance:** This paper has important implications for the deployment of LLMs in real-world applications, especially those dealing with scientific knowledge and high-stakes decisions. If reasoning leads to overconfidence without improved accuracy, it's a serious risk. The finding that RAG improves calibration offers a concrete direction for future research and development. The significance is further enhanced by the focus on expert confidence, which is a crucial aspect often overlooked in other calibration studies.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Empirical Rigor:** The study uses established datasets (CLIMATEX, IARC), well-defined metrics (accuracy, Cohen's Kappa), and controls (RoBERTa baseline, "The Onion" dataset for external validity, prompt robustness checks). This lends credibility to the findings.
    *   **Clear Research Questions:** The paper clearly articulates its research questions and directly addresses them through experimentation.
    *   **Counterintuitive Result:** The main finding is surprising and challenges conventional wisdom about LLM scaling.
    *   **Practical Implications:** The emphasis on RAG as a solution has direct practical value.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope of Domains:** While expanding to the health domain, the results might not generalize to all knowledge-intensive tasks.  Further investigation across different scientific fields is warranted.
    *   **Limited Human Baselines:** The study acknowledges the limitations of the existing non-expert human baseline and the lack of expert human baseline for the CLIMATEX task. This limits the ability to fully contextualize the LLM performance.
    *   **Specific Models and Datasets:** The experiments are primarily conducted using specific LLMs (Gemini family) and datasets (CLIMATEX, IARC), which means the results might be specific to the models and datasets used. While the Gemini models tested are widely used, the conclusions would be strengthened by testing a greater variety of architectures.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence future research by:
    *   Shifting the focus from simply scaling LLMs to improving information retrieval and synthesis.
    *   Encouraging more research on confidence calibration in knowledge-intensive tasks.
    *   Highlighting the importance of domain-specific datasets and expert annotations for evaluating LLM performance.
    *   Making developers more cautious about relying solely on reasoning abilities without grounding them in verifiable evidence.

*   **Justification for Score:** The paper delivers a valuable and counterintuitive finding that prompts a re-evaluation of how we approach LLM development for knowledge-intensive tasks.  However, the limitations concerning domain specificity and baselines prevent a higher score.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### S3LoRA: Safe Spectral Sharpness-Guided Pruning in Adaptation of Agent Planner
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15068v1)
- **Authors**: Shuang Ao, Gopal Rumchurn
- **Abstract**: Adapting Large Language Models (LLMs) using parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) techniques such as LoRA has enabled powerful capabilities in LLM-based agents. However, these adaptations can unintentionally compromise safety alignment, leading to unsafe or unstable behaviors, particularly in agent planning tasks. Existing safety-aware adaptation methods often require access to both base and instruction-tuned model checkpoints, which are frequently unavailable in practice, limiting their applicability. We propose S3LoRA (Safe Spectral Sharpness-Guided Pruning LoRA), a lightweight, data-free, and model-independent framework that mitigates safety risks in LoRA-adapted models by inspecting only the fine-tuned weight updates. We first introduce Magnitude-Aware Spherically Normalized SVD (MAS-SVD), which robustly analyzes the structural properties of LoRA updates while preserving global magnitude information. We then design the Spectral Sharpness Index (SSI), a sharpness-aware metric to detect layers with highly concentrated and potentially unsafe updates. These layers are pruned post-hoc to reduce risk without sacrificing task performance. Extensive experiments and ablation studies across agent planning and language generation tasks show that S3LoRA consistently improves safety metrics while maintaining or improving utility metrics and significantly reducing inference cost. These results establish S3LoRA as a practical and scalable solution for safely deploying LLM-based agents in real-world, resource-constrained, and safety-critical environments.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces S³LORA (Safe Spectral Sharpness-Guided Pruning LoRA), a data-free and model-independent framework designed to mitigate safety risks in LoRA-adapted large language models (LLMs), particularly in agent planning tasks. S³LORA analyzes fine-tuned weight updates using Magnitude-Aware Spherically Normalized SVD (MAS-SVD) to identify layers with highly concentrated and potentially unsafe updates, which are then pruned. This process aims to improve safety metrics without sacrificing task performance. The paper includes extensive experiments across agent planning and language generation tasks, demonstrating that S³LORA improves safety while maintaining or improving utility and reducing inference costs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its data-free and model-independent approach to safety alignment in LoRA-adapted LLMs, focusing solely on fine-tuned weight updates. The MAS-SVD method and Spectral Sharpness Index (SSI) offer a novel way to analyze and identify potentially problematic layers without needing the original base model or instruction-tuned checkpoints, which is a key advantage over existing methods like SafeLoRA and SPLORA. The combination of magnitude-aware normalization and spectral analysis appears to be a unique contribution.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the work stems from its potential to address the growing concerns about safety alignment in LLM-based agents, especially in planning tasks. By providing a practical and scalable solution that can be deployed in resource-constrained and safety-critical environments, the paper tackles a pressing problem in the field. The fact that it does not rely on potentially unavailable resources like base or instruction-tuned models significantly broadens its applicability. The reported improvements in both safety and efficiency are significant and could have a notable impact on how LLM agents are deployed. However, the experiments primarily focus on a set of tasks, and further validation across a broader range of safety scenarios is necessary to fully establish its robustness. Also, the tuning of the pruning threshold could limit its application across different task types and models.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The method is data-free and model-independent, making it widely applicable.
    *   MAS-SVD and SSI provide a novel way to analyze LoRA updates.
    *   Experiments demonstrate improved safety and utility.
    *   Reduces inference cost through pruning.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The pruning threshold is fixed and may require tuning for different tasks.
    *   The correlation between spectral sharpness and safety is assumed and may not hold in all scenarios.
    *   Experiments are limited to a few specific tasks and models.
    *   The full list of refusal keywords not provided.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by offering a practical and scalable solution for safety alignment in LLM-based agents. The MAS-SVD and SSI techniques could be adopted by other researchers and practitioners, leading to safer and more efficient LLM deployments. The paper encourages further research into data-free methods for safety alignment and spectral analysis of model weights.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of safety alignment in LLMs. The data-free and model-independent nature of S³LORA is a major strength. While the experimental results are promising, the method requires more extensive validation across diverse tasks and models to fully demonstrate its robustness. The fixed pruning threshold and assumptions about the correlation between spectral sharpness and safety represent limitations that need to be addressed in future work. Nevertheless, the paper's potential influence and practical value justify a score of 7. It makes a solid advancement but can be significantly improved.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### CurveFlow: Curvature-Guided Flow Matching for Image Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15093v1)
- **Authors**: Yan Luo, Drake Du, Hao Huang, Yi Fang, Mengyu Wang
- **Abstract**: Existing rectified flow models are based on linear trajectories between data and noise distributions. This linearity enforces zero curvature, which can inadvertently force the image generation process through low-probability regions of the data manifold. A key question remains underexplored: how does the curvature of these trajectories correlate with the semantic alignment between generated images and their corresponding captions, i.e., instructional compliance? To address this, we introduce CurveFlow, a novel flow matching framework designed to learn smooth, non-linear trajectories by directly incorporating curvature guidance into the flow path. Our method features a robust curvature regularization technique that penalizes abrupt changes in the trajectory's intrinsic dynamics.Extensive experiments on MS COCO 2014 and 2017 demonstrate that CurveFlow achieves state-of-the-art performance in text-to-image generation, significantly outperforming both standard rectified flow variants and other non-linear baselines like Rectified Diffusion. The improvements are especially evident in semantic consistency metrics such as BLEU, METEOR, ROUGE, and CLAIR. This confirms that our curvature-aware modeling substantially enhances the model's ability to faithfully follow complex instructions while simultaneously maintaining high image quality. The code is made publicly available at https://github.com/Harvard-AI-and-Robotics-Lab/CurveFlow.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CurveFlow, a novel flow-matching framework designed to improve image generation by incorporating curvature guidance into the flow path between data and noise distributions. Current rectified flow models use linear trajectories, which the authors argue can force the image generation process through low-probability regions of the data manifold and hinder semantic alignment with text prompts. CurveFlow addresses this by learning smooth, non-linear trajectories by directly incorporating curvature guidance into the flow. It uses a robust curvature regularization technique to penalize abrupt changes in trajectory dynamics. The authors demonstrate state-of-the-art performance in text-to-image generation on MS COCO 2014 and 2017, outperforming standard rectified flow variants and other non-linear baselines, particularly in semantic consistency metrics. The paper emphasizes that curvature-aware modeling enhances the model's ability to faithfully follow complex instructions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper's primary novelty lies in the explicit incorporation of curvature guidance into flow matching for image generation.  While prior works have explored non-linear trajectories, this paper provides a specific and technically detailed approach to achieve this by learning a parameterized curve with smoothness regularization. The introduction of the `Lrobust_curvature` loss is another novel contribution, aiming to stabilize training by directly penalizing abrupt changes in the scaling functions. The analysis of how linear trajectories might lead to semantic inconsistencies is also a valuable contribution.

**Significance:**

The improved performance on semantic consistency metrics (BLEU, METEOR, ROUGE, CLAIR) is significant. It provides empirical evidence that carefully modeling the trajectory between noise and data distributions can improve a generative model's ability to follow complex instructions. The superior FID scores also suggest an improvement in image quality. The quantitative and qualitative results showcase the advantages of CurveFlow over existing methods, demonstrating its potential impact on text-to-image generation tasks.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of linear trajectories in rectified flow models.
*   **Well-Defined Approach:** CurveFlow is well-defined and technically sound. The curvature regularization technique is a valuable addition.
*   **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper provides comprehensive experimental results on a standard benchmark dataset, demonstrating the effectiveness of CurveFlow over existing methods.
*   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies regarding the regularization parameter and network dimensions offer insights into the model's behavior and optimal configuration.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Computational Cost:** While the paper claims a favorable trade-off, the fact remains that CurveFlow requires more computational resources than RF w/o Reweighting. A more detailed analysis of the computational overhead would be beneficial.
*   **Complexity:**  The added complexity of the model is a factor. While the authors justify it with improved performance, it must be acknowledged.
*  **Model size is a factor:** While the parameters introduced via LoRA are acceptable, future models could target lower parameter costs with high quality and fidelity.
*   **Limited comparison in one-step performance:** Some of the more recent approaches have targeted single-step generation. Comparing in that context would provide additional insight.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a significant contribution to the field of generative modeling. The explicit incorporation of curvature guidance in flow matching, along with the robust regularization technique, addresses a critical limitation of existing methods. The strong empirical results demonstrate the practical benefits of CurveFlow, particularly in improving semantic consistency in text-to-image generation. The paper is well-written, technically sound, and provides valuable insights into the relationship between trajectory geometry and image generation quality. The quantitative gains over current methods are real, as is the improvement in instructional compliance. Therefore a score of 8/10 is warranted.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Evaluating Sparse Autoencoders for Monosemantic Representation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15094v1)
- **Authors**: Moghis Fereidouni, Muhammad Umair Haider, Peizhong Ju, A. B. Siddique
- **Abstract**: A key barrier to interpreting large language models is polysemanticity, where neurons activate for multiple unrelated concepts. Sparse autoencoders (SAEs) have been proposed to mitigate this issue by transforming dense activations into sparse, more interpretable features. While prior work suggests that SAEs promote monosemanticity, there has been no quantitative comparison with their base models. This paper provides the first systematic evaluation of SAEs against base models concerning monosemanticity. We introduce a fine-grained concept separability score based on the Jensen-Shannon distance, which captures how distinctly a neuron's activation distributions vary across concepts. Using Gemma-2-2B and multiple SAE variants across five benchmarks, we show that SAEs reduce polysemanticity and achieve higher concept separability. However, greater sparsity of SAEs does not always yield better separability and often impairs downstream performance. To assess practical utility, we evaluate concept-level interventions using two strategies: full neuron masking and partial suppression. We find that, compared to base models, SAEs enable more precise concept-level control when using partial suppression. Building on this, we propose Attenuation via Posterior Probabilities (APP), a new intervention method that uses concept-conditioned activation distributions for targeted suppression. APP outperforms existing approaches in targeted concept removal.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a systematic evaluation of Sparse Autoencoders (SAEs) in promoting monosemanticity within large language models (LLMs).  It introduces a novel concept separability score based on Jensen-Shannon distance to quantify how distinctly a neuron's activations vary across different concepts. Using Gemma-2-2B and various SAE variants, the authors demonstrate that SAEs reduce polysemanticity and achieve higher concept separability compared to base models.  The study also investigates the impact of SAE sparsity on both concept separability and downstream performance, finding that greater sparsity doesn't always yield better separability and can impair performance.  Finally, the paper proposes a new intervention method, Attenuation via Posterior Probabilities (APP), for targeted concept removal, outperforming existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's primary strength lies in its quantitative evaluation of SAEs for monosemanticity. Previous work has largely relied on qualitative assessments or case studies. The introduction of the Jensen-Shannon distance-based concept separability score is a significant contribution, as it offers a more fine-grained, distribution-aware metric compared to simple overlap statistics. The APP intervention method is also novel, leveraging concept-conditioned activation distributions for targeted suppression. However, some existing techniques use distributionally-aware approaches. The core idea of using sparsity to promote monosemanticity has been explored previously.

* **Significance:** The findings have implications for the interpretability and controllability of LLMs. By providing quantitative evidence that SAEs promote monosemantic representations, the paper strengthens the argument for using SAEs as a tool for understanding model behavior. The APP intervention method offers a potentially more precise and effective way to control LLMs at the concept level. The observation that excessive sparsity can be detrimental is also valuable, highlighting the need for careful tuning of SAE parameters. The comprehensive evaluation across multiple datasets and SAE variants adds to the robustness of the findings.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Quantitative Evaluation:** Moves beyond qualitative assessments of SAEs.
    * **Novel Metric:** Introduces a fine-grained concept separability score.
    * **Novel Intervention Method:** Proposes APP for targeted concept removal.
    * **Comprehensive Experiments:** Evaluates across multiple datasets and SAE variants.
    * **Practical Implications:** Offers insights into improving the interpretability and controllability of LLMs.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Scope:** The study focuses primarily on Gemma-2-2B. While this is a reasonable starting point, evaluating on other LLMs would strengthen the generalizability of the findings.
    * **Computational Cost:**  The increased computational cost of SAEs compared to base models is mentioned but not thoroughly addressed in terms of the trade-offs involved. Quantifying the computational overhead more precisely and exploring methods to mitigate it would enhance the practical value of the work.
    * **Incremental Improvement:** While APP outperforms other intervention methods, the relative improvements in some cases might be considered incremental rather than revolutionary. A more detailed analysis of the scenarios where APP provides the most significant advantage would be beneficial.
    * **Clarity**: In some parts, details are hidden away and thus make it difficult to discern the significance.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research in the area of LLM interpretability and control. The concept separability score could become a standard metric for evaluating monosemanticity. The APP intervention method could inspire new approaches to targeted concept removal.  The finding about the trade-offs between sparsity and performance is also likely to guide future work on SAE parameter tuning. Overall, the paper provides valuable insights and tools for advancing the field.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper makes a solid contribution with its quantitative evaluation of SAEs and the introduction of the concept separability score and APP intervention method. While the core idea of using sparsity to promote monosemanticity is not entirely new, the thorough evaluation and novel contributions justify a score of 7. It has a real impact on the community and will inform the research efforts in the domain. The limitations regarding the scope (single LLM), computational cost analysis, and incremental improvements of APP prevent a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Nemotron-CC-Math: A 133 Billion-Token-Scale High Quality Math Pretraining Dataset
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15096v1)
- **Authors**: Rabeeh Karimi Mahabadi, Sanjeev Satheesh, Shrimai Prabhumoye, Mostofa Patwary, Mohammad Shoeybi, Bryan Catanzaro
- **Abstract**: Pretraining large language models (LLMs) on high-quality, structured data such as mathematics and code substantially enhances reasoning capabilities. However, existing math-focused datasets built from Common Crawl suffer from degraded quality due to brittle extraction heuristics, lossy HTML-to-text conversion, and the failure to reliably preserve mathematical structure. In this work, we introduce Nemotron-CC-Math, a large-scale, high-quality mathematical corpus constructed from Common Crawl using a novel, domain-agnostic pipeline specifically designed for robust scientific text extraction. Unlike previous efforts, our pipeline recovers math across various formats (e.g., MathJax, KaTeX, MathML) by leveraging layout-aware rendering with lynx and a targeted LLM-based cleaning stage. This approach preserves the structural integrity of equations and code blocks while removing boilerplate, standardizing notation into LaTeX representation, and correcting inconsistencies. We collected a large, high-quality math corpus, namely Nemotron-CC-Math-3+ (133B tokens) and Nemotron-CC-Math-4+ (52B tokens). Notably, Nemotron-CC-Math-4+ not only surpasses all prior open math datasets-including MegaMath, FineMath, and OpenWebMath-but also contains 5.5 times more tokens than FineMath-4+, which was previously the highest-quality math pretraining dataset. When used to pretrain a Nemotron-T 8B model, our corpus yields +4.8 to +12.6 gains on MATH and +4.6 to +14.3 gains on MBPP+ over strong baselines, while also improving general-domain performance on MMLU and MMLU-Stem. We present the first pipeline to reliably extract scientific content--including math--from noisy web-scale data, yielding measurable gains in math, code, and general reasoning, and setting a new state of the art among open math pretraining corpora. To support open-source efforts, we release our code and datasets.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the Nemotron-CC-Math paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Nemotron-CC-Math, a new large-scale (133B tokens) high-quality mathematical pretraining dataset built from Common Crawl data. The authors address the shortcomings of existing math datasets, which often suffer from degraded quality due to brittle extraction heuristics, lossy HTML-to-text conversion, and failure to preserve mathematical structure.  They present a novel, domain-agnostic pipeline leveraging layout-aware rendering with lynx and LLM-based cleaning to extract and standardize math notation in ATEX format. The dataset surpasses previous open math datasets in size and quality, leading to significant performance gains when used to pretrain a Nemotron-T 8B model on benchmarks like MATH and MBPP+, while also improving general-domain performance. The authors release their code and datasets to encourage open-source research.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the robust extraction pipeline specifically designed for scientific content from Common Crawl. While Common Crawl has been used before, the authors' technique to preserve mathematical structure using Lynx and then cleaning with an LLM is a significant improvement over existing math dataset creation techniques that rely on more brittle, heuristic-based methods. The paper explicitly points out that older techniques can drop equations and fail to extract content appropriately. The method addresses a longstanding challenge in web-scale data processing and achieves measurable gains on multiple tasks. The release of the pipeline itself is a contribution, potentially benefiting other fields beyond math pretraining. However, the use of an LLM-based cleaner is not revolutionary, and similar techniques are increasingly used for data cleaning and processing.

* **Significance:** The paper is significant because it provides a large, high-quality, and openly available math pretraining dataset, a critical resource for advancing mathematical reasoning in LLMs. The impressive performance improvements demonstrated on MATH and MBPP+ solidify the practical value of their dataset. The release of both the dataset and the pipeline promotes reproducibility and further research in this area. The generalizability of the extraction pipeline to other scientific domains adds to the paper's impact. The ablation study provides a good analysis of model choice, and overall, the experiments are well justified and thorough.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Robust Extraction Pipeline:** The Lynx + LLM cleaning pipeline demonstrably improves data quality and addresses major problems with prior approaches.
    * **Dataset Size and Quality:** Nemotron-CC-Math significantly increases the amount of high-quality open math pretraining data.
    * **Empirical Validation:** The experiments clearly demonstrate performance gains on relevant benchmarks.
    * **Open Source Release:** Promotes reproducibility and future research.
    * **Generalizability:** The pipeline is designed to be domain-agnostic, potentially benefiting other scientific fields.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **LLM-based Cleaning is Not Entirely Novel:** Using LLMs for data cleaning is becoming more common, diminishing the novelty of this component. Although their implementation may still be innovative, the overall approach has become a common theme in research.
    * **Reliance on Common Crawl:** Although the pipeline itself addresses some Common Crawl deficiencies, a dependency remains on its overall quality and representativeness.
    * **Limited Analysis:**  While the data analysis in Section 3.3 is good, there are not any other detailed examples in the paper showcasing the shortcomings of existing techniques, other than general qualitative descriptions in Appendix A.

* **Potential Influence:** The dataset and pipeline have the potential to become widely adopted in the math pretraining community. The improved performance on benchmarks could lead to more capable LLMs for math and related fields.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the strengths and weaknesses, I assign a score of **8**.  The paper makes a substantial contribution by addressing a key challenge in creating high-quality math pretraining data. The novel pipeline, leading to significant performance gains and dataset release, is well-validated empirically and has a notable impact on open-source research. While the LLM-based cleaning is less groundbreaking on its own, its integration into a broader robust pipeline is novel, useful, and well-motivated.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### LLMs and Agentic AI in Insurance Decision-Making: Opportunities and Challenges For Africa
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15110v1)
- **Authors**: Graham Hill, JingYuan Gong, Thulani Babeli, Moseli Mots'oehli, James Gachomo Wanjiku
- **Abstract**: In this work, we highlight the transformative potential of Artificial Intelligence (AI), particularly Large Language Models (LLMs) and agentic AI, in the insurance sector. We consider and emphasize the unique opportunities, challenges, and potential pathways in insurance amid rapid performance improvements, increased open-source access, decreasing deployment costs, and the complexity of LLM or agentic AI frameworks. To bring it closer to home, we identify critical gaps in the African insurance market and highlight key local efforts, players, and partnership opportunities. Finally, we call upon actuaries, insurers, regulators, and tech leaders to a collaborative effort aimed at creating inclusive, sustainable, and equitable AI strategies and solutions: by and for Africans.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the potential of Large Language Models (LLMs) and agentic AI in transforming the insurance sector in Africa. It highlights the unique opportunities and challenges specific to the African context, considering factors like rapid technological advancements, open-source access, deployment costs, and the complexity of AI frameworks. The authors identify gaps in the African insurance market, showcasing local efforts, players, and partnership possibilities. They emphasize the importance of collaboration between actuaries, insurers, regulators, and tech leaders to create inclusive, sustainable, and equitable AI solutions tailored for Africans. The paper reviews AI concepts, global use cases, and African initiatives, addressing the crucial relationship between technology adoption and regulation within the financial market. Finally, it calls for a concerted effort to develop and maintain AI-based insurance solutions specifically designed for the African market, leveraging local data and expertise.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper addresses a relevant and timely topic.  The focus on the African insurance market is a significant strength, as this region often gets overlooked in discussions about AI in finance. However, there are some limitations in its novelty.

**Strengths:**

*   **Focus on Africa:** The paper's primary strength lies in its specific focus on the African context, which is often neglected in broader discussions about AI and insurance. This provides a valuable perspective on the unique challenges and opportunities present in the region.
*   **Comprehensive Overview:** The paper provides a good overview of LLMs and agentic AI, along with their application in insurance. It also covers global use cases and specific examples of AI deployments in Africa.
*   **Emphasis on Collaboration:** The call for collaboration between various stakeholders (actuaries, insurers, regulators, and tech leaders) is a crucial point, as it highlights the need for a holistic approach to AI adoption.
*   **Consideration of Policy and Governance:** The inclusion of a section on policy and governance is essential, as regulatory frameworks play a critical role in shaping the adoption and impact of AI.
*   **Local examples** The paper gives examples of African AI and LLM companies with different origins, their main roles, and technologies.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Lack of Technical Depth:** While the paper provides a good overview of LLMs and agentic AI, it lacks detailed technical explanations. A more in-depth discussion of the specific AI techniques used in the cited African initiatives would enhance the paper's value.
*   **Limited Empirical Evidence:** The paper relies primarily on existing literature and reports. Including original empirical data or case studies would strengthen its arguments.
*   **Superficial analysis:** Some of the companies mentioned are superficially discussed and no detailed insights are provided.
*   **Novelty:** The overall contribution to the field is limited in its novelty. The work mostly summarizes existing knowledge and highlights the importance of applying AI to the African insurance sector. The recommendations are not particularly new or groundbreaking.

**Significance:**

Despite its limitations, the paper is significant because it brings attention to the potential of AI in the African insurance market. It serves as a valuable starting point for further research and discussion in this area.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering both the strengths and weaknesses, the paper's novelty is limited. It does not present any new algorithms, models, or empirical findings. However, its focus on Africa and its call for collaboration make it a valuable contribution to the discussion of AI in insurance. Therefore, a score of 5 is justified.

### Side Effects of Erasing Concepts from Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15124v1)
- **Authors**: Shaswati Saha, Sourajit Saha, Manas Gaur, Tejas Gokhale
- **Abstract**: Concerns about text-to-image (T2I) generative models infringing on privacy, copyright, and safety have led to the development of Concept Erasure Techniques (CETs). The goal of an effective CET is to prohibit the generation of undesired ``target'' concepts specified by the user, while preserving the ability to synthesize high-quality images of the remaining concepts. In this work, we demonstrate that CETs can be easily circumvented and present several side effects of concept erasure. For a comprehensive measurement of the robustness of CETs, we present Side Effect Evaluation (\see), an evaluation benchmark that consists of hierarchical and compositional prompts that describe objects and their attributes. This dataset and our automated evaluation pipeline quantify side effects of CETs across three aspects: impact on neighboring concepts, evasion of targets, and attribute leakage. Our experiments reveal that CETs can be circumvented by using superclass-subclass hierarchy and semantically similar prompts, such as compositional variants of the target. We show that CETs suffer from attribute leakage and counterintuitive phenomena of attention concentration or dispersal. We release our dataset, code, and evaluation tools to aid future work on robust concept erasure.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Side Effects of Erasing Concepts from Diffusion Models" addresses the problem of unintended consequences when using Concept Erasure Techniques (CETs) in text-to-image (T2I) diffusion models. It introduces a new benchmark, Side Effect Evaluation (SEE), to evaluate the robustness of CETs across three aspects: impact on neighboring concepts, evasion of targets, and attribute leakage. The SEE dataset contains compositional text prompts describing objects and their attributes, facilitating a systematic evaluation. The paper evaluates four state-of-the-art CET methods on the Stable Diffusion model and reveals vulnerabilities, showing that CETs struggle with compositional concepts, exhibit limited generalization across semantic hierarchies, and suffer from attribute leakage. The paper also identifies artifacts in T2I diffusion models, such as attention dispersal, and demonstrates that progressive erasure is more effective than all-at-once erasure.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its comprehensive and systematic approach to evaluating CETs. While prior work has explored concept erasure, this paper is the first to thoroughly quantify *side effects* and create a dedicated benchmark (SEE) to analyze these effects across multiple dimensions (neighboring concepts, evasion, attribute leakage). The identification of attention dispersal as an artifact during unsuccessful erasure adds another layer of insight. The analysis of progressive versus all-at-once erasure is also novel.  While existing works focused on the accuracy of erasing the target concept and the accuracy of preserving unrelated concepts, this work investigates what happens to *related* concepts, how erasure can be *evaded*, and the *leakage of attributes* from the erased concept.

* **Significance:** The work is significant because it highlights the limitations of current CETs and raises concerns about their safety and efficacy. The findings reveal that CETs can be easily circumvented and can unintentionally affect semantically adjacent concepts. This is crucial information for developers and users of T2I models, as it points to the need for more robust and reliable concept erasure techniques. The public release of the SEE dataset and evaluation tools provides a valuable resource for future research in this area. The findings suggest that more sophisticated methods are needed that go beyond simple accuracy metrics and address hierarchical and compositional understanding. The results also suggest that attention mechanisms need to be carefully calibrated to prevent attribute leakage.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The SEE benchmark provides a rigorous and multifaceted evaluation of CETs, going beyond simple accuracy metrics.
    * **Systematic Approach:** The paper follows a clear methodology and conducts large-scale experiments to quantify side effects.
    * **Practical Relevance:** The findings have practical implications for the development and deployment of safe and reliable T2I models.
    * **Resource Contribution:** The public release of the SEE dataset, code, and evaluation tools supports future research.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Scope of "Concepts":**  The definition of "concept" is restricted to object categories and supercategories. Expanding the scope to include styles, people, or more abstract concepts would make the evaluation more comprehensive. The paper also acknowledges this limitation.
    * **Evaluation Metrics Limitations:** The work relies heavily on CLIP and VQA models for automated evaluation. While these are standard tools, they have limitations in their understanding of nuanced visual content, and may not perfectly capture the presence or absence of concepts.  The paper addresses this by using multiple VQA models.
    * **Limited Set of CETs:** While the paper evaluates four CETs, there are other existing techniques that were not evaluated.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to have a significant influence on the field of T2I models and concept erasure. It provides a valuable benchmark for evaluating the robustness of CETs and identifies key challenges that need to be addressed. It should spur further research on developing more sophisticated and reliable concept erasure techniques that minimize unintended side effects. It might also influence the development of safer training datasets.

* **Justification of Score:** The paper makes a significant contribution by highlighting the shortcomings of existing CETs and providing a robust framework for evaluating their side effects. While there are some limitations in the scope of concepts and evaluation metrics, the paper's novelty, significance, and practical relevance warrant a high score.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### aiXiv: A Next-Generation Open Access Ecosystem for Scientific Discovery Generated by AI Scientists
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15126v1)
- **Authors**: Pengsong Zhang, Xiang Hu, Guowei Huang, Yang Qi, Heng Zhang, Xiuxu Li, Jiaxing Song, Jiabin Luo, Yijiang Li, Shuo Yin, Chengxiao Dai, Eric Hanchen Jiang, Xiaoyan Zhou, Zhenfei Yin, Boqin Yuan, Jing Dong, Guinan Su, Guanren Qiao, Haiming Tang, Anghong Du, Lili Pan, Zhenzhong Lan, Xinyu Liu
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in large language models (LLMs) have enabled AI agents to autonomously generate scientific proposals, conduct experiments, author papers, and perform peer reviews. Yet this flood of AI-generated research content collides with a fragmented and largely closed publication ecosystem. Traditional journals and conferences rely on human peer review, making them difficult to scale and often reluctant to accept AI-generated research content; existing preprint servers (e.g. arXiv) lack rigorous quality-control mechanisms. Consequently, a significant amount of high-quality AI-generated research lacks appropriate venues for dissemination, hindering its potential to advance scientific progress. To address these challenges, we introduce aiXiv, a next-generation open-access platform for human and AI scientists. Its multi-agent architecture allows research proposals and papers to be submitted, reviewed, and iteratively refined by both human and AI scientists. It also provides API and MCP interfaces that enable seamless integration of heterogeneous human and AI scientists, creating a scalable and extensible ecosystem for autonomous scientific discovery. Through extensive experiments, we demonstrate that aiXiv is a reliable and robust platform that significantly enhances the quality of AI-generated research proposals and papers after iterative revising and reviewing on aiXiv. Our work lays the groundwork for a next-generation open-access ecosystem for AI scientists, accelerating the publication and dissemination of high-quality AI-generated research content. Code is available at https://github.com/aixiv-org. Website is available at https://forms.gle/DxQgCtXFsJ4paMtn8.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper collection based on your criteria:

**Overall Summary:**

The collection presents several research papers focused on advancing scientific discovery through the use of AI, particularly Large Language Models (LLMs) and multi-agent systems. The core theme revolves around automating scientific processes, improving publication ecosystems, and enhancing the performance of various machine learning models.  The papers cover diverse topics, including automated experimentation, scientific proposal generation, enhancing mode capture in diffusion models, and boosting grokking in mathematical operations. All papers make the claim to contribute to the research community.

**Critical Evaluation of Novelty and Significance:**

The *aiXiv: A Next-Generation Open Access Ecosystem for Scientific Discovery* paper tackles a significant problem: the bottleneck in disseminating AI-generated research. The concept of a platform specifically designed for AI scientists to submit, review, and refine scientific content is novel and could potentially accelerate scientific progress. However, the success of this platform hinges on the robustness of its AI-driven review processes and its ability to filter out low-quality or fabricated research. Given that much of the underlying work relies on existing LLM techniques, its core novelty comes from combining these elements into a cohesive ecosystem. *Score: 7*

The work in *DUALDIFF: ENHANCING MODE CAPTURE IN LOW-DIMENSIONAL DIFFUSION MODELS VIA DUAL-EXPERT DENOISING* is focused on improving the performance of diffusion models in low-dimensional settings. While diffusion models are already well-established, enhancing their ability to accurately capture complex distributions in these settings is relevant for scientific simulations and other applications. The dual-expert denoising architecture is a potentially valuable contribution, although the improvements observed were not universally substantial across all datasets. *Score: 6*

*GAN-ENHANCED DIFFUSION: BOOSTING SAMPLE QUALITY AND DIVERSITY* combines GANs and Diffusion models. The paper aims to boost the capabilities of Diffusion models, by overcoming limitations in sample quality. *Score: 7*

*ACCELERATING MATHEMATICAL INSIGHT: BOOSTING GROKKING THROUGH STRATEGIC DATA AUGMENTATION* explores how data augmentation can enhance "grokking," where models suddenly generalize. The combined augmentation strategy shows promise in accelerating mathematical operation learning. Its a bit narrow in focus, but its useful to the grokking dynamic. *Score: 6*

**Overall Assessment:**

The collection demonstrates the potential of AI-driven approaches to transform various aspects of scientific research and machine learning. While most of the papers present incremental improvements to existing techniques, *aiXiv* stands out for its potential to significantly impact the way scientific research is disseminated and evaluated in the age of AI. The remaining papers are solid, but their success hinges on the broader impact to the current communities.
