### Identifying and Answering Questions with False Assumptions: An Interpretable Approach
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15139v1)
- **Authors**: Zijie Wang, Eduardo Blanco
- **Abstract**: People often ask questions with false assumptions, a type of question that does not have regular answers. Answering such questions require first identifying the false assumptions. Large Language Models (LLMs) often generate misleading answers because of hallucinations. In this paper, we focus on identifying and answering questions with false assumptions in several domains. We first investigate to reduce the problem to fact verification. Then, we present an approach leveraging external evidence to mitigate hallucinations. Experiments with five LLMs demonstrate that (1) incorporating retrieved evidence is beneficial and (2) generating and validating atomic assumptions yields more improvements and provides an interpretable answer by specifying the false assumptions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of answering questions containing false assumptions, where Large Language Models (LLMs) often provide misleading answers due to hallucinations rather than identifying the incorrect assumption. The authors propose an interpretable approach that leverages external evidence to mitigate these hallucinations and provide better answers. Their method involves generating and validating atomic assumptions, which are then used to identify and address the question's false premises. The approach is evaluated on three existing datasets, (QA)2, CREPE, and FalseQA, demonstrating its effectiveness compared to existing methods and providing interpretable answers by pinpointing the specific false assumptions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the interpretable approach to answering questions with false assumptions. While using retrieval-augmented methods is not novel in itself, the generation and validation of *atomic* assumptions to provide a *specific*, human-readable explanation of *why* a question is based on a false premise is a valuable contribution. Previous methods often generate answers in an end-to-end fashion without providing this level of interpretability. It differs from fact verification by not simply looking for contradictions, but rather identifying underlying misconceptions.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant limitation of LLMs, which is their tendency to hallucinate and generate misleading answers when faced with questions based on false premises. Improving the reliability and interpretability of LLMs in such scenarios is crucial for real-world applications where users might not be aware of the underlying false assumptions in their queries. This work can contribute to building more robust and trustworthy QA systems. By identifying these false assumptions, the system can prevent users from getting stuck in faulty thinking.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Interpretable Answers:** The generation and validation of atomic assumptions provide a clear, human-readable explanation for why a question is unanswerable. This is a significant advantage over previous end-to-end approaches.
    *   **State-of-the-art Results:** The approach achieves competitive or state-of-the-art results on all three benchmark datasets, demonstrating its effectiveness.
    *   **Unified Approach:** The paper presents a unified approach that works across different datasets, whereas previous work was often dataset-specific.
    *   **Error Analysis:** The error analysis provides valuable insights into the limitations of the approach and areas for future improvement, such as retrieving more relevant evidence.
    *   **Computational Efficiency:** Claiming lower computation cost is a nice addition.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on LLMs:** The approach heavily relies on LLMs for generating and validating atomic assumptions, meaning the performance is still limited by the capabilities of the LLM. Hallucinations, while mitigated, are not completely eliminated. It depends on the LLM for every component of the pipeline.
    *   **Limited Scope of False Assumptions:** The paper defines false assumptions as subjective opinions, beliefs, or misconceptions. There might be other types of false assumptions that are not captured by this definition.
    *   **Annotation Requirement:** Although providing interpretability is a strength, the reliance on human annotation to validate the assumptions in the questions and their atomic interpretations can be a bottleneck. Automating this part of the pipeline would further improve its practicality.

*   **Potential Influence:** The work can potentially influence the field of QA systems by promoting the development of more interpretable and reliable models. The approach of generating and validating atomic assumptions can be adopted in other areas of NLP where interpretability is crucial, such as fact verification and explainable AI.

*   **Justification of Score:** Given the novel interpretable approach, competitive results, and the significance of addressing LLM hallucinations, the paper makes a solid contribution to the field. However, the reliance on LLMs, limited scope of false assumptions, and need for human validation slightly limit its impact.

### QueryGenie: Making LLM-Based Database Querying Transparent and Controllable
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15146v1)
- **Authors**: Longfei Chen, Shenghan Gao, Shiwei Wang, Ken Lin, Yun Wang, Quan Li
- **Abstract**: Conversational user interfaces powered by large language models (LLMs) have significantly lowered the technical barriers to database querying. However, existing tools still encounter several challenges, such as misinterpretation of user intent, generation of hallucinated content, and the absence of effective mechanisms for human feedback-all of which undermine their reliability and practical utility. To address these issues and promote a more transparent and controllable querying experience, we proposed QueryGenie, an interactive system that enables users to monitor, understand, and guide the LLM-driven query generation process. Through incremental reasoning, real-time validation, and responsive interaction mechanisms, users can iteratively refine query logic and ensure alignment with their intent.
- **Summary**: **Summary:**

The paper introduces QueryGenie, an interactive system designed to enhance the transparency and controllability of database querying using Large Language Models (LLMs). QueryGenie addresses issues like misinterpretation of user intent, hallucination, and lack of user feedback mechanisms in existing LLM-based querying tools. The system adopts a three-stage framework: Intention Confirmation, Query Generation, and Query Validation. It visualizes the mapping between user input and database schema, uses chain-of-thought (CoT) for query generation with step-by-step explanations, and allows users to validate and edit SQL statements in real-time. A preliminary user study suggests that QueryGenie improves accuracy compared to Vanna, although it requires more time, and users appreciate the increased transparency and control.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper's novelty lies primarily in its interaction framework for LLM-based data querying, emphasizing transparency and user control. While the individual components (schema linking visualization, CoT reasoning, SQL validation) are not entirely novel, their integration into a cohesive interactive system for database querying constitutes a valuable contribution. Existing approaches mainly focus on improving model performance through prompt engineering or fine-tuning, whereas QueryGenie directly tackles user trust and understanding by making the LLM's reasoning process visible and editable. The tree-based visualization of query logic and the color-coded SQL structure are also potentially useful interaction techniques.

**Significance:**

The significance of the paper stems from its potential to improve the reliability and usability of LLM-based data querying tools. By addressing the black-box nature of LLMs and empowering users to understand and guide the query generation process, QueryGenie can increase user confidence and promote the adoption of these tools in practical settings.  The empirical evaluation, although preliminary, provides initial evidence of QueryGenie's effectiveness in enhancing accuracy. However, the study is limited by its small sample size and a relatively narrow task domain. The findings suggest a promising direction for future research on human-AI collaboration in data querying. A significant limitation is the exclusive reliance on GPT-4. The paper would benefit from showing how its tool would perform against a spectrum of models to prove its worth.

**Strengths:**

*   The paper addresses a crucial problem in LLM-based data querying: the lack of transparency and control.
*   The interaction framework is well-designed and integrates several potentially useful techniques.
*   The preliminary evaluation provides initial evidence of QueryGenie's effectiveness.
*   The user feedback highlights the system's usability and user satisfaction.
* The paper is well written and explains the concepts clearly.

**Weaknesses:**

*   The empirical evaluation is limited by its small sample size and narrow task domain.
*   The comparison is limited to Vanna. It could benefit from including other baselines.
*   The paper lacks a detailed analysis of the types of errors that QueryGenie can effectively address.
*   Scalability and performance issues for large databases are not discussed.
* The tool does not address or evaluate diverse datasets, schemas, and models.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence the field of human-AI collaboration in data querying by demonstrating the benefits of transparency and user control. It can inspire further research on interactive systems that empower users to understand and guide the reasoning process of LLMs. The interaction techniques introduced in the paper, such as the schema linking visualization and the tree-based query logic representation, can be adapted and extended to other domains. However, more extensive evaluation is needed to confirm the long-term impact of QueryGenie.

**Rigorous Rationale for the Score:**

Given the paper's novelty in integrating existing interaction techniques into a cohesive framework for transparent and controllable LLM-based data querying, its potential to improve user trust and reliability, and the initial evidence of effectiveness from the preliminary evaluation, a score reflecting a solid, but not groundbreaking, contribution is warranted. While the individual components are not entirely new, their combination and application to the specific problem domain is novel. However, the limited empirical evaluation and the lack of a deep dive into the types of errors that can be effectively corrected limit the score. It could benefit from more robust empirical evidence.

**Score: 6.5**

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 6/10

### Zero-shot Volumetric CT Super-Resolution using 3D Gaussian Splatting with Upsampled 2D X-ray Projection Priors
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15151v1)
- **Authors**: Jeonghyun Noh, Hyun-Jic Oh, Byungju Chae, Won-Ki Jeong
- **Abstract**: Computed tomography (CT) is widely used in clinical diagnosis, but acquiring high-resolution (HR) CT is limited by radiation exposure risks. Deep learning-based super-resolution (SR) methods have been studied to reconstruct HR from low-resolution (LR) inputs. While supervised SR approaches have shown promising results, they require large-scale paired LR-HR volume datasets that are often unavailable. In contrast, zero-shot methods alleviate the need for paired data by using only a single LR input, but typically struggle to recover fine anatomical details due to limited internal information. To overcome these, we propose a novel zero-shot 3D CT SR framework that leverages upsampled 2D X-ray projection priors generated by a diffusion model. Exploiting the abundance of HR 2D X-ray data, we train a diffusion model on large-scale 2D X-ray projection and introduce a per-projection adaptive sampling strategy. It selects the generative process for each projection, thus providing HR projections as strong external priors for 3D CT reconstruction. These projections serve as inputs to 3D Gaussian splatting for reconstructing a 3D CT volume. Furthermore, we propose negative alpha blending (NAB-GS) that allows negative values in Gaussian density representation. NAB-GS enables residual learning between LR and diffusion-based projections, thereby enhancing high-frequency structure reconstruction. Experiments on two datasets show that our method achieves superior quantitative and qualitative results for 3D CT SR.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel zero-shot 3D CT super-resolution (SR) framework designed to generate high-resolution CT volumes from low-resolution CT scans, leveraging upsampled 2D X-ray projection priors. The method uses a pre-trained diffusion model to enhance 2D X-ray projections and incorporates a per-projection adaptive sampling strategy to improve HR upsampling.  For 3D reconstruction, the authors employ 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) and introduce a new module called Negative Alpha Blending Gaussian Splatting (NAB-GS) to enable residual learning between the original LR projections and the diffusion-enhanced HR projections. This NAB-GS extension allows the representation of negative density values, which the authors argue is essential for effective residual learning. Experiments on two datasets demonstrate superior quantitative and qualitative results compared to existing 3D CT SR methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates a degree of novelty in its approach to zero-shot 3D CT SR. The use of pre-trained diffusion models for X-ray projection upsampling isn't entirely new in itself. However, combining it with 3DGS and the proposed NAB-GS is novel. The per-projection adaptive sampling strategy is also a useful contribution.  The introduction of NAB-GS to handle negative density values in 3DGS for residual learning seems to be a major contribution in volumetric SR. This adaptation is significant because it addresses a fundamental limitation of standard 3DGS in effectively representing residuals.

* **Significance:** The paper tackles an important problem in medical imaging: improving CT image resolution while minimizing radiation exposure. The zero-shot approach is appealing because it reduces reliance on paired LR-HR CT datasets, which are often scarce. Successfully integrating upsampled 2D X-ray projections represents a significant step, as 2D X-ray data is more readily available. The performance improvements shown in the experiments are promising and suggest that the method could have a practical impact. The NAB-GS module could be adopted more widely in future works for volumetric reconstruction.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly outlines the limitations of existing CT SR methods and the motivation for a zero-shot approach utilizing 2D X-ray priors.
    * **Technical Soundness:** The methodology is well-explained, with detailed descriptions of the diffusion model, 3DGS, PAS, and NAB-GS components. The ablation studies demonstrate the impact of each component, strengthening the claims.
    * **Experimental Validation:** The experiments on two publicly available datasets provide robust evidence of the method's effectiveness. The quantitative results (PSNR, SSIM) consistently outperform existing methods, and the qualitative results visually confirm the improvements in anatomical detail and noise reduction.
    * **Practical Relevance:** The method addresses a real-world problem in clinical imaging and offers a potentially more accessible solution due to the reliance on 2D X-ray data.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on Pre-trained Model:** The method depends on a pre-trained diffusion model, which may introduce biases or limitations depending on the training data.  More discussion on the characteristics of the pre-trained data, how its performance might vary with different anatomies, and its robustness would be beneficial. The method relies on the availability of HR X-ray images; it should be explicit about how its performance degrades if the HR X-rays used to train the prior are of lower quality than the output CT volume.
    * **Limited View Consistency:** The paper notes in Section 6 that its pre-trained diffusion prior, is "limited to frontal and lateral views" suggesting the lack of consistency of the upsampled 2D projections across the full angular range, which may lead to inconsistencies in the reconstructed 3D volume. This could be a significant bottleneck, requiring further investigation of the method with different angle of 2D X-ray.
    * **Parameter Sensitivity:** The negative slope (γ) parameter in NAB-GS is shown to be sensitive and dataset-specific, which may require careful tuning in practice. The parameter tuning and potentially long convergence time of GS are practical concerns.
    * **Limited Comparisons:** The comparison could be improved by including other recent zero-shot or unsupervised SR methods, if any exist for 3D medical volumes. A runtime or memory consumption comparison would also enhance the analysis.

* **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of medical image SR. The zero-shot approach and the use of widely available 2D X-ray data make the method highly practical. The improved resolution and noise reduction could lead to better diagnostic accuracy and reduced radiation exposure for patients. The NAB-GS module can potentially stimulate new research directions to improve volumetric SR.

**Justification of Score:**

Considering the novelty of the overall framework and the significant contributions of NAB-GS and the adaptive sampling strategy, along with the solid experimental results and potential for practical impact, the paper warrants a high score. However, the limitations regarding the dependency of pretrained model, sensitivity of parameter tuning, consistency across viewing angles, and scope for more comprehensive experimental comparison detract from it being exceptional.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: eess.IV
- **Score**: 8/10

### ContextualLVLM-Agent: A Holistic Framework for Multi-Turn Visually-Grounded Dialogue and Complex Instruction Following
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15164v1)
- **Authors**: Seungmin Han, Haeun Kwon, Ji-jun Park, Taeyang Yoon
- **Abstract**: Despite significant advancements in Large Language Models (LLMs) and Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs), current models still face substantial challenges in handling complex, multi-turn, and visually-grounded tasks that demand deep reasoning, sustained contextual understanding, entity tracking, and multi-step instruction following. Existing benchmarks often fall short in capturing the dynamism and intricacies of real-world multi-modal interactions, leading to issues such as context loss and visual hallucinations. To address these limitations, we introduce MMDR-Bench (Multi-Modal Dialogue Reasoning Benchmark), a novel dataset comprising 300 meticulously designed complex multi-turn dialogue scenarios, each averaging 5-7 turns and evaluated across six core dimensions including visual entity tracking and reasoning depth. Furthermore, we propose CoLVLM Agent (Contextual LVLM Agent), a holistic framework that enhances existing LVLMs with advanced reasoning and instruction following capabilities through an iterative "memory-perception-planning-execution" cycle, requiring no extensive re-training of the underlying models. Our extensive experiments on MMDR-Bench demonstrate that CoLVLM Agent consistently achieves superior performance, attaining an average human evaluation score of 4.03, notably surpassing state-of-the-art commercial models like GPT-4o (3.92) and Gemini 1.5 Pro (3.85). The framework exhibits significant advantages in reasoning depth, instruction adherence, and error suppression, and maintains robust performance over extended dialogue turns, validating the effectiveness of its modular design and iterative approach for complex multi-modal interactions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ContextualLVLM-Agent (CoLVLM Agent), a novel framework designed to improve the performance of Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs) in complex multi-turn, visually-grounded dialogues and instruction following tasks. It addresses the limitations of existing models in handling long-term memory, contextual understanding, and multi-step instructions.  CoLVLM Agent operates through an iterative "memory-perception-planning-execution" cycle, and the authors propose MMDR-Bench, a new benchmark dataset, to evaluate these capabilities. Experimental results on MMDR-Bench demonstrate that CoLVLM Agent outperforms state-of-the-art commercial and open-source LVLMs across several metrics. Ablation studies validate the contribution of individual modules within the framework.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addresses a Significant Problem:** The paper tackles a well-defined and crucial challenge: the limitations of LVLMs in handling complex, multi-turn interactions that require deep reasoning and contextual understanding. This is a significant barrier to real-world deployment.
    *   **Novel Framework:**  The CoLVLM Agent framework offers a plausible and structured approach. The "memory-perception-planning-execution" cycle is a logical organization, and the individual modules (Dialogue Context Memory, Dynamic Visual Perception, Reasoning & Planning, Action Execution) seem well-suited to their respective tasks. It is good that the approach requires no re-training of underlying LVLMs.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The authors present a thorough evaluation with a new, expertly-curated dataset (MMDR-Bench). Comparing against commercial (GPT-40, Gemini 1.5 Pro) and open-source (LLaVA-1.5, Qwen-VL-Plus) models adds credibility. The use of human evaluators alongside LLM-based evaluation is sound. The ablation study clearly demonstrates the importance of each module.
    *   **Clear Results:** The performance gains of CoLVLM Agent over baselines are statistically significant, particularly in areas like Reasoning Depth, Instruction Adherence, and Error Suppression. The detailed error analysis provides useful insights into the framework's advantages.
    *   **MMDR-Bench contribution**: The proposal of this benchmark addresses the limitation of the other VQA dataset, which usually focuses on single-turn Visual Question Answering

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Computational Overhead:** The higher latency of the CoLVLM Agent due to its modular nature is a significant drawback for real-time applications. The authors acknowledge this, but it limits practical adoption.
    *   **Dependency on Underlying LVLM:** While avoiding retraining is an advantage, the CoLVLM Agent's performance is still heavily dependent on the capabilities of the underlying LVLM. This could limit its effectiveness if used with a weaker base model.
    *   **Limited Generalizability?** The MMDR-Bench dataset, while well-designed, may not fully capture the diversity of real-world scenarios.  The framework's performance on other datasets or in different application domains needs further validation.
    *   **Incremental Innovation:** While the architecture is well-structured, the innovation might be seen as somewhat incremental, building on existing modular approaches to multi-modal reasoning. The modules themselves are not drastically new ideas, but the overall system integration is where novelty lies.

*   **Significance:**

    The paper holds considerable significance. It moves beyond simply scaling up models and instead focuses on architectural improvements for complex multi-modal interactions. The MMDR-Bench dataset is a valuable contribution to the research community, providing a challenging benchmark for future LVLM development. The modular design of CoLVLM Agent offers a flexible and adaptable framework that can be integrated with various LVLMs. The paper can be seen as a promising step towards building more capable and human-like AI agents that can seamlessly interact with the real world through vision and language. However, the high latency and reliance on strong underlying LVLMs could be limiting factors for near-term applications.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the strengths and weaknesses, I assign a score of **7**.

The paper demonstrates solid contributions by proposing a novel architecture to mitigate the limitations of existing LVLMs and also the proposal of a novel benchmark addresses the limitations of the existing VQA dataset. The design choices appear sound, and the evaluation is comprehensive. However, the high latency of the proposed methods need to be addressed to enable real-world applications, and further benchmark and more detailed experiments should be conducted to demonstrate general applicability.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### MeSS: City Mesh-Guided Outdoor Scene Generation with Cross-View Consistent Diffusion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15169v1)
- **Authors**: Xuyang Chen, Zhijun Zhai, Kaixuan Zhou, Zengmao Wang, Jianan He, Dong Wang, Yanfeng Zhang, mingwei Sun, Rüdiger Westermann, Konrad Schindler, Liqiu Meng
- **Abstract**: Mesh models have become increasingly accessible for numerous cities; however, the lack of realistic textures restricts their application in virtual urban navigation and autonomous driving. To address this, this paper proposes MeSS (Meshbased Scene Synthesis) for generating high-quality, styleconsistent outdoor scenes with city mesh models serving as the geometric prior. While image and video diffusion models can leverage spatial layouts (such as depth maps or HD maps) as control conditions to generate street-level perspective views, they are not directly applicable to 3D scene generation. Video diffusion models excel at synthesizing consistent view sequences that depict scenes but often struggle to adhere to predefined camera paths or align accurately with rendered control videos. In contrast, image diffusion models, though unable to guarantee cross-view visual consistency, can produce more geometry-aligned results when combined with ControlNet. Building on this insight, our approach enhances image diffusion models by improving cross-view consistency. The pipeline comprises three key stages: first, we generate geometrically consistent sparse views using Cascaded Outpainting ControlNets; second, we propagate denser intermediate views via a component dubbed AGInpaint; and third, we globally eliminate visual inconsistencies (e.g., varying exposure) using the GCAlign module. Concurrently with generation, a 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) scene is reconstructed by initializing Gaussian balls on the mesh surface. Our method outperforms existing approaches in both geometric alignment and generation quality. Once synthesized, the scene can be rendered in diverse styles through relighting and style transfer techniques.
- **Summary**: The paper "MeSS: City Mesh-Guided Outdoor Scene Generation with Cross-View Consistent Diffusion" introduces a novel pipeline for generating realistic, high-quality Gaussian Splatting scenes from textureless urban meshes. The method leverages diffusion models, constrained by city mesh models to improve geometric consistency and visual appearance. The core contribution lies in a cascaded outpainting ControlNet approach for generating consistent keyframes, an appearance-guided inpainting method (AGInpaint) to densify intermediate views, and a global consistency alignment module (GCAlign) to address visual inconsistencies. The paper claims state-of-the-art performance in both geometric alignment and generation quality, while maintaining cross-view consistency. The method also demonstrates the ability to render synthesized scenes in diverse styles through relighting and style transfer techniques.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper combines several existing techniques (ControlNets, Gaussian Splatting, diffusion models) in a novel way tailored to the specific problem of city scene generation from meshes. The cascaded outpainting approach with ControlNets aims to address long-range consistency issues that are common in existing perpetual view generation methods. The introduction of AGInpaint is also a good contribution that specifically targets silhouette removal and texture filling. GCAlign is helpful for reducing the effect of inconsistent camera calibration or lighting effects. Overall, the individual components are not entirely new, but their specific combination and adaptation for this problem are significant.

**Significance:** The problem addressed (generating realistic city scenes from readily available mesh models) is relevant and impactful. The ability to create visually compelling and geometrically accurate scenes has applications in virtual navigation, autonomous driving simulation, and urban planning visualization. The paper demonstrates quantitative improvements over existing methods in both geometric alignment and generation quality, which makes it likely the method represents a genuine advancement.

**Strengths:**

*   Addresses a practical and relevant problem.
*   Combines existing techniques in a novel and effective manner.
*   Introduces specific components (Cascaded ControlNets, AGInpaint, GCAlign) tailored to the task.
*   Demonstrates quantitative and qualitative improvements over existing methods.
*   Offers a flexible pipeline for generating scenes and enabling stylistic variations through rendering.

**Weaknesses:**

*   The paper relies on several well-known components, which means the incremental novelty of any single part might be considered moderate.
*   Comparisons to some existing works are challenging due to the lack of publicly available code or differences in training datasets.
*   The improvements are, in some areas, incremental, which is particularly evident from the FID/KID score.
*   Results are currently limited to the City Sample data, so there's no clear indication of the performance in different locations with potentially varying quality of meshes.
*   Ablation study for GCAlign shows it does achieve view harmonization, but introduces blurring effect, the trade-off should be addressed and/or mitigated.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to the field of scene generation by addressing a practical problem with a well-designed and technically sound approach. While the individual components are not radically new, their integration and optimization within the MeSS pipeline represent a significant advancement over existing techniques. The demonstrated improvements in geometric accuracy and visual quality, along with the flexibility offered by the pipeline, make this a valuable contribution. However, the reliance on established methods and some limitations in generality, as discussed above, prevent it from scoring higher. The score reflects the balance between the paper's strengths (novel combination, practical relevance, demonstrated improvements) and weaknesses (reliance on existing methods, comparison limitations).

### PuzzleClone: An SMT-Powered Framework for Synthesizing Verifiable Data
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15180v1)
- **Authors**: Kai Xiong, Yanwei Huang, Rongjunchen Zhang, Kun Chen, Haipang Wu
- **Abstract**: High-quality mathematical and logical datasets with verifiable answers are essential for strengthening the reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs). While recent data augmentation techniques have facilitated the creation of large-scale benchmarks, existing LLM-generated datasets often suffer from limited reliability, diversity, and scalability. To address these challenges, we introduce PuzzleClone, a formal framework for synthesizing verifiable data at scale using Satisfiability Modulo Theories (SMT). Our approach features three key innovations: (1) encoding seed puzzles into structured logical specifications, (2) generating scalable variants through systematic variable and constraint randomization, and (3) ensuring validity via a reproduction mechanism. Applying PuzzleClone, we construct a curated benchmark comprising over 83K diverse and programmatically validated puzzles. The generated puzzles span a wide spectrum of difficulty and formats, posing significant challenges to current state-of-the-art models. We conduct post training (SFT and RL) on PuzzleClone datasets. Experimental results show that training on PuzzleClone yields substantial improvements not only on PuzzleClone testset but also on logic and mathematical benchmarks. Post training raises PuzzleClone average from 14.4 to 56.2 and delivers consistent improvements across 7 logic and mathematical benchmarks up to 12.5 absolute percentage points (AMC2023 from 52.5 to 65.0). Our code and data are available at https://github.com/puzzleclone.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PuzzleClone, a novel SMT-powered framework for synthesizing verifiable mathematical and logical puzzles. The framework encodes seed puzzles into structured logical specifications, generates scalable variants through variable and constraint randomization, and ensures validity via a reproduction mechanism using the Z3 SMT solver. The authors created a curated benchmark of over 83K diverse puzzles and demonstrated that training large language models (LLMs) on PuzzleClone datasets significantly improves their reasoning capabilities, not only on the PuzzleClone test set but also on established logic and mathematical benchmarks. Post training the Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct improves from 14.4 to 56.2 and achieves gains of up to 12.5 absolute percentage points across 7 logic and mathematical benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel approach to data augmentation using SMT solvers to generate verifiable and diverse reasoning datasets. The key innovation lies in the structured encoding and systematic randomization of seed puzzles, ensuring both scalability and data reliability. This is a significant departure from existing data augmentation techniques that heavily rely on LLMs for annotation and verification, which can introduce biases and inaccuracies.
*   **Significance:** The PuzzleClone framework addresses a crucial need in the field of LLM research: the lack of high-quality, diverse, and scalable datasets for training and evaluating reasoning capabilities. The curated benchmark poses significant challenges to current state-of-the-art models, indicating its potential to drive further advancements in the field. The demonstrated improvements in LLM performance after training on PuzzleClone datasets highlight the effectiveness of the proposed approach.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Formal framework with verifiable data synthesis.
    *   Scalability and diversity of the generated dataset.
    *   Significant improvements in LLM reasoning capabilities after training on PuzzleClone.
    *   Comprehensive evaluation on both PuzzleClone and established benchmarks.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The difficulty score has limitations as the non-linear relationship between the conditional and increased difficulties are not captured. Also, the `var_scale` metric estimates how variable domains affect difficulty, but it uses a simplistic diff factor with only three discrete values to represent correlation direction.
    *   The reliance on manual encoding of seed puzzles might limit the scalability of the framework. Automatically parsing the puzzle text to generate formal specs could overcome this limitation.
    *   The specification expressiveness is limited to linear optimization.

*   **Potential Influence:** The PuzzleClone framework has the potential to become a valuable tool for researchers and practitioners working on LLM reasoning. The benchmark and the data synthesis approach can be used to train more robust and reliable models, as well as to evaluate their capabilities in a controlled and verifiable manner. The work could also inspire further research on formal methods for data augmentation and the development of more sophisticated reasoning benchmarks.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

The novelty of PuzzleClone lies in its use of SMT-based data augmentation, a departure from LLM-dependent methods. Its significance is evident in the substantial performance gains on both the PuzzleClone benchmark and other reasoning datasets. While the reliance on manual seed puzzle encoding poses a scalability challenge, the framework's design and the benchmark's impact justify a high score.

### SafeLLM: Unlearning Harmful Outputs from Large Language Models against Jailbreak Attacks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15182v1)
- **Authors**: Xiangman Li, Xiaodong Wu, Qi Li, Jianbing Ni, Rongxing Lu
- **Abstract**: Jailbreak attacks pose a serious threat to the safety of Large Language Models (LLMs) by crafting adversarial prompts that bypass alignment mechanisms, causing the models to produce harmful, restricted, or biased content. In this paper, we propose SafeLLM, a novel unlearning-based defense framework that unlearn the harmful knowledge from LLMs while preserving linguistic fluency and general capabilities. SafeLLM employs a three-stage pipeline: (1) dynamic unsafe output detection using a hybrid approach that integrates external classifiers with model-internal evaluations; (2) token-level harmful content tracing through feedforward network (FFN) activations to localize harmful knowledge; and (3) constrained optimization to suppress unsafe behavior without degrading overall model quality. SafeLLM achieves targeted and irreversible forgetting by identifying and neutralizing FFN substructures responsible for harmful generation pathways. Extensive experiments on prominent LLMs (Vicuna, LLaMA, and GPT-J) across multiple jailbreak benchmarks show that SafeLLM substantially reduces attack success rates while maintaining high general-purpose performance. Compared to standard defense methods such as supervised fine-tuning and direct preference optimization, SafeLLM offers stronger safety guarantees, more precise control over harmful behavior, and greater robustness to unseen attacks. Moreover, SafeLLM maintains the general performance after the harmful knowledge unlearned. These results highlight unlearning as a promising direction for scalable and effective LLM safety.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "SafeLLM: Unlearning Harmful Outputs from Large Language Models against Jailbreak Attacks":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SafeLLM, a novel framework designed to defend Large Language Models (LLMs) against jailbreak attacks. These attacks manipulate LLMs into generating harmful, restricted, or biased content by bypassing safety alignment mechanisms. SafeLLM operates in three stages: 1) Dynamic detection of unsafe outputs using a combination of external classifiers and internal model evaluations.  2) Token-level tracing of harmful content through Feedforward Neural Network (FFN) activations to pinpoint the location of harmful knowledge within the model. 3)  Constrained optimization to suppress unsafe behavior without sacrificing the LLM's overall performance. The method claims to achieve targeted and irreversible forgetting of harmful behaviors. Experiments on various LLMs (Vicuna, LLaMA, GPT-J) demonstrate that SafeLLM effectively reduces attack success rates while maintaining general-purpose performance. The paper compares SafeLLM to standard defense methods like supervised fine-tuning and direct preference optimization, claiming stronger safety guarantees, precise control, and robustness.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its integration of dynamic harmful output detection with a token-level unlearning mechanism. While the individual components (toxicity scoring, FFN analysis, constrained optimization) are not entirely new, their combination into a comprehensive framework specifically targeting jailbreak attacks represents a significant step forward. The method's claim of "irreversible" forgetting, achieved through pinpointing and neutralizing FFN substructures, is another potentially novel aspect. Howevr the exact novelty should be compared to other unlearning methods in this space such as SKU.

*   **Significance:**  The significance of this work stems from the pressing need to address the security vulnerabilities of LLMs, particularly in the face of increasingly sophisticated jailbreak attacks. The ability to selectively "unlearn" harmful knowledge without compromising overall model performance is a valuable contribution to the field of trustworthy AI. The proposed framework has the potential to enhance the safety and reliability of LLMs across a range of applications.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper presents a well-defined and clearly articulated framework with a logical flow from dynamic detection to token-level unlearning and constrained optimization.
    *   The experimental results on multiple LLMs and jailbreak benchmarks support the effectiveness of SafeLLM in reducing attack success rates.
    *   The comparative analysis with other defense methods highlights the advantages of SafeLLM in terms of safety guarantees and control over harmful behavior.
    *   The paper offers valuable insights into the internal workings of LLMs, particularly the role of FFN activations in generating harmful content.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The claim of "irreversible" forgetting requires further scrutiny. While the paper demonstrates a reduction in attack success rates, it's crucial to verify that the harmful behaviors cannot be reactivated through alternative adversarial prompts or by exploiting different model vulnerabilities. The term "irreversible" might be an overstatement.
    *   The complexity of the SafeLLM framework may limit its scalability and applicability to larger LLMs or resource-constrained environments. The computational cost of token-level tracing and constrained optimization needs to be carefully considered.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more in-depth discussion of the limitations of SafeLLM. For example, it would be useful to explore the types of harmful knowledge that are most difficult to unlearn, or the specific scenarios in which the framework may be less effective.
    *   The selection of specific jailbreak methods may not be exhaustive, and further testing against a wider range of attack strategies is warranted.
    *   The "fine-grained control" might introduce subtle biases, as what is considered "harmful" is subjective. Further study is needed in this direction.

*   **Potential Influence:** SafeLLM has the potential to significantly influence the development of more robust and trustworthy LLMs. Its token-level unlearning mechanism provides a promising direction for mitigating the risks associated with jailbreak attacks. The framework's insights into FFN activations and harmful knowledge generation could also inform the design of safer and more controllable LLM architectures. The work could also stimulate further research into the area of machine unlearning for LLMs.

*   **Score Justification:**
    The paper presents a significant contribution to the field of LLM security, demonstrating a practical and effective framework for mitigating jailbreak attacks. The token-level unlearning mechanism is a particularly promising aspect, offering a more targeted and controllable approach to removing harmful knowledge. However, the claim of "irreversible" forgetting needs further validation, and the scalability of the framework remains a concern. A deeper discussion of the framework's limitations and potential biases would also enhance the paper's credibility.
    Based on these considerations, the paper merits a score of:

### SemToken: Semantic-Aware Tokenization for Efficient Long-Context Language Modeling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15190v1)
- **Authors**: Dong Liu, Yanxuan Yu
- **Abstract**: Tokenization plays a critical role in language modeling, yet existing approaches such as Byte-Pair Encoding (BPE) or WordPiece operate purely on frequency statistics, ignoring the underlying semantic structure of text. This leads to over-tokenization of semantically redundant spans and underutilization of contextual coherence, particularly in long-context scenarios. In this work, we propose \textbf{SemToken}, a semantic-aware tokenization framework that jointly reduces token redundancy and improves computation efficiency. SemToken first extracts contextual semantic embeddings via lightweight encoders and performs local semantic clustering to merge semantically equivalent tokens. Then, it allocates heterogeneous token granularity based on semantic density, allowing finer-grained tokenization in content-rich regions and coarser compression in repetitive or low-entropy spans. SemToken can be seamlessly integrated with modern language models and attention acceleration methods. Experiments on long-context language modeling benchmarks such as WikiText-103 and LongBench show that SemToken achieves up to $2.4\times$ reduction in token count and $1.9\times$ speedup, with negligible or no degradation in perplexity and downstream accuracy. Our findings suggest that semantic structure offers a promising new axis for optimizing tokenization and computation in large language models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the SemToken paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SemToken, a semantic-aware tokenization framework designed to improve the efficiency of long-context language models.  Unlike traditional tokenizers (BPE, WordPiece) that rely solely on frequency statistics, SemToken analyzes the semantic content of text to reduce token redundancy and improve computational efficiency. It works in two stages: 1) extracting contextual semantic embeddings via lightweight encoders and clustering them to merge semantically equivalent tokens, and 2) allocating heterogeneous token granularity based on semantic density (finer-grained in content-rich regions, coarser in repetitive regions). The authors demonstrate that SemToken can significantly reduce token count and inference latency with minimal impact on perplexity and downstream accuracy across various long-context benchmarks.  The framework is designed to be model-agnostic and can be easily integrated with existing LLMs and attention acceleration techniques.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of incorporating semantic information into the tokenization process to reduce redundancy is novel. While techniques like token merging exist, SemToken takes a more structured and principled approach by explicitly considering semantic density and entropy. The two-stage process – clustering for redundancy reduction and adaptive granularity – is a key distinguishing factor. The combination of these techniques, resulting in overall benefits, enhances the paper's contribution.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial challenge in the scaling of LLMs: the computational cost associated with long context lengths. By reducing the number of tokens processed without significantly impacting performance, SemToken provides a valuable tool for improving the efficiency of LLMs in applications like document understanding, dialogue, and retrieval-augmented generation. Reducing memory usage is of utmost importance for deploying large language models, especially for inference purposes. The impact may be further amplified by compounding benefits alongside efficient attention mechanisms and caching strategies.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The paper presents extensive experimental results on standard long-context benchmarks, demonstrating significant gains in token efficiency, latency, and memory usage across different architectures and attention variants.
    *   **Model Agnostic:** SemToken is designed to be a plug-and-play component that can be integrated with existing LLMs without retraining.
    *   **Detailed Ablation Studies:** Ablation experiments isolate the contribution of each module (clustering, density scoring, AR-budgeting), providing insights into the framework's design choices.
    *   **Comprehensive Visualizations:** The paper includes insightful visualizations of semantic density patterns, token compression trajectories, and cluster distributions, which help to understand the behavior of SemToken.
    *   **Analysis of theoretical efficiency gain:** The authors quantify their approach, which makes it stronger.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Encoder Choice:** The paper mentions using SimCSE or distilled BERT as lightweight encoders. More detailed analysis of how the encoder selection impacts performance would be beneficial, as this is a critical design choice. The authors should explore other encoder variants.
    *   **Threshold Tuning:** The approach relies on similarity threshold (T) and entropy threshold (δ). While the experiments show positive results, the paper should include a sensitivity analysis of these parameters.
    *   **Computational Overhead of SemToken:** While the paper demonstrates end-to-end speedups, it would be useful to quantify the computational cost specifically associated with the SemToken pipeline (embedding, clustering, granularity assignment) to fully assess its practical benefits.
    *   **Limited Scope of Future Directions:** It would be interesting to see the authors expand on their future research, e.g., by focusing on particular applications, such as reinforcement learning.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence research in several directions:
    *   **Tokenization Strategies:**  It motivates further exploration of semantic-aware tokenization techniques for improving LLM efficiency.
    *   **Efficient Inference:** It highlights the importance of addressing the tokenization bottleneck in long-context models, complementing existing efforts on attention acceleration and caching.
    *   **Resource-Constrained Deployment:** It provides a valuable tool for deploying LLMs in resource-constrained environments (e.g., edge devices) where token efficiency is critical.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and well-validated approach to semantic-aware tokenization.  The demonstrated improvements in token efficiency, latency, and memory usage are significant and relevant to the broader LLM community. While the paper has some limitations (encoder choice, threshold tuning, and computational overhead), the overall contribution is substantial and warrants a relatively high score. It could be improved by addressing the listed weaknesses.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### LLM4Sweat: A Trustworthy Large Language Model for Hyperhidrosis Support
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15192v1)
- **Authors**: Wenjie Lin, Jin Wei-Kocsis
- **Abstract**: While large language models (LLMs) have shown promise in healthcare, their application for rare medical conditions is still hindered by scarce and unreliable datasets for fine-tuning. Hyperhidrosis, a disorder causing excessive sweating beyond physiological needs, is one such rare disorder, affecting 2-3% of the population and significantly impacting both physical comfort and psychosocial well-being. To date, no work has tailored LLMs to advance the diagnosis or care of hyperhidrosis. To address this gap, we present LLM4Sweat, an open-source and domain-specific LLM framework for trustworthy and empathetic hyperhidrosis support. The system follows a three-stage pipeline. In the data augmentation stage, a frontier LLM generates medically plausible synthetic vignettes from curated open-source data to create a diverse and balanced question-answer dataset. In the fine-tuning stage, an open-source foundation model is fine-tuned on the dataset to provide diagnosis, personalized treatment recommendations, and empathetic psychological support. In the inference and expert evaluation stage, clinical and psychological specialists assess accuracy, appropriateness, and empathy, with validated responses iteratively enriching the dataset. Experiments show that LLM4Sweat outperforms baselines and delivers the first open-source LLM framework for hyperhidrosis, offering a generalizable approach for other rare diseases with similar data and trustworthiness challenges.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the LLM4Sweat paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LLM4Sweat, an open-source LLM framework specifically designed to provide trustworthy support for individuals suffering from hyperhidrosis (excessive sweating). It addresses the challenge of limited and unreliable data for rare conditions like hyperhidrosis by employing a three-stage pipeline: 1) *Data Augmentation:*  A frontier LLM generates medically plausible synthetic vignettes to create a diverse and balanced question-answer dataset from limited open-source data. 2) *Fine-tuning:*  An open-source foundation model is fine-tuned on this augmented dataset. 3) *Inference and Expert Evaluation:* Clinical and psychological specialists evaluate the LLM's outputs for accuracy, appropriateness, and empathy, iteratively enriching the dataset with validated responses.  Experiments demonstrate that LLM4Sweat outperforms baseline models in diagnosis, treatment recommendations, and psychosocial support, offering a generalizable approach for other rare diseases with similar data challenges.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its focused application of LLMs to a rare and under-resourced medical condition, hyperhidrosis.  While the individual components (data augmentation, fine-tuning, expert-in-the-loop evaluation) are not entirely novel on their own, their combination and tailoring to address the specific challenges of rare disease support represent a unique contribution. The explicit focus on trustworthiness and empathetic support is also a differentiating factor. Previous work in medical LLMs often focus on common conditions, making this condition-specific approach novel.

* **Significance:** The paper is significant for several reasons:
    *   **Addressing a Gap:** It directly tackles the lack of reliable and personalized medical support for hyperhidrosis, a condition affecting a substantial portion of the population but often overlooked.
    *   **Generalizable Framework:** The proposed framework is not limited to hyperhidrosis. It offers a practical and scalable approach for developing trustworthy LLMs for other rare diseases characterized by data scarcity and a need for empathetic support.
    *   **Emphasis on Trustworthiness:** The expert-in-the-loop evaluation and iterative refinement process prioritize accuracy, appropriateness, and empathy, crucial for deploying LLMs in sensitive medical contexts. This goes beyond simply optimizing for performance metrics.
    *   **Open-Source Contribution:** By releasing the framework as open-source, the authors facilitate further research, development, and adaptation to other rare medical conditions.
    *   **Results Validations**: The paper claims superiority over baseline LLMs on metrics of accuracy, precision and recall in the diagnosis and treatment of hyperhidrosis.

* **Strengths:**
    *   **Well-defined Problem:** The paper clearly articulates the challenge of data scarcity in rare diseases and its impact on the trustworthiness of medical LLMs.
    *   **Comprehensive Approach:** The three-stage pipeline is well-designed and addresses the key challenges in developing reliable LLMs for rare conditions.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of LLM4Sweat compared to baseline models, and highlight the contributions of individual components through ablation studies.
    *   **Emphasis on Practical Deployment:**  The paper outlines a clear path to deployment in real-world applications, considering both technical and ethical considerations.

* **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Evaluation Scope:** The evaluation relies on a relatively small dataset (80 multiple-choice questions) and a limited number of expert evaluation cycles. While the results are promising, more extensive validation is needed to ensure robustness and generalizability. The model is only tested on 80 questions.
    *   **Reliance on Frontier LLM for Data Augmentation:**  The quality of the synthetic data generated by the frontier LLM (GPT-5) is crucial to the success of the framework. Although the authors filter for medical plausibility, there is still a risk of introducing biases or inaccuracies into the training data. The quality is directly reliant on the frontier LLM.
    *   **Simulated User Interaction:** The interactions are simulated, and the "users" are responding to multiple-choice questions, not free-form text.
    *   **Lack of Deeper Analysis of Errors:** The paper presents performance metrics, but it lacks a deeper qualitative analysis of the types of errors made by the models and the reasons behind them. This would provide valuable insights for further improvement.
    *   **Limited discussion of ethical considerations**: The paper includes one sentence about standards of care and regulation, which is not enough.

* **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence the development of LLMs for rare diseases and other specialized domains. It provides a practical and adaptable framework that can be used by researchers and developers to address the challenges of data scarcity and trustworthiness. The focus on empathetic support is also likely to inspire further research on incorporating emotional intelligence into medical LLMs.

**Justification for Score:**

The LLM4Sweat paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of medical LLMs, particularly in the context of rare diseases. While there are some limitations in the evaluation scope and potential biases in the synthetic data, the strengths of the paper outweigh these weaknesses. The proposed framework offers a practical and generalizable approach for developing trustworthy and empathetic LLMs, and the experimental results demonstrate its effectiveness. I am assigning a score of 7 based on:
*   **Novelty of Application:** The focus on hyperhidrosis is a distinctive application. (+1)
*   **Practical Framework:** A clearly defined, multi-stage framework for data augmentation, fine-tuning, and expert evaluation that can be implemented. (+1)
*   **Emphasis on Trustworthiness:** The expert-in-the-loop approach enhances the reliability of the model. (+1)
*   **Significant Performance Gains:** LLM4Sweat demonstrates significant improvements over baseline models. (+2)
*   **Limitations in Validation and Scope:** The relatively small test data set of 80 questions and the use of GPT-5 for generating a dataset (+1)
*  **Lack of Ethcial considerations** (+0.5)
*  **Lack of error analysis** (+0.5)

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Fin-PRM: A Domain-Specialized Process Reward Model for Financial Reasoning in Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15202v1)
- **Authors**: Yuanchen Zhou, Shuo Jiang, Jie Zhu, Junhui Li, Lifan Guo, Feng Chen, Chi Zhang
- **Abstract**: Process Reward Models (PRMs) have emerged as a promising framework for supervising intermediate reasoning in large language models (LLMs), yet existing PRMs are primarily trained on general or Science, Technology, Engineering, and Mathematics (STEM) domains and fall short in domain-specific contexts such as finance, where reasoning is more structured, symbolic, and sensitive to factual and regulatory correctness. We introduce \textbf{Fin-PRM}, a domain-specialized, trajectory-aware PRM tailored to evaluate intermediate reasoning steps in financial tasks. Fin-PRM integrates step-level and trajectory-level reward supervision, enabling fine-grained evaluation of reasoning traces aligned with financial logic. We apply Fin-PRM in both offline and online reward learning settings, supporting three key applications: (i) selecting high-quality reasoning trajectories for distillation-based supervised fine-tuning, (ii) providing dense process-level rewards for reinforcement learning, and (iii) guiding reward-informed Best-of-N inference at test time. Experimental results on financial reasoning benchmarks, including CFLUE and FinQA, demonstrate that Fin-PRM consistently outperforms general-purpose PRMs and strong domain baselines in trajectory selection quality. Downstream models trained with Fin-PRM yield substantial improvements with baselines, with gains of 12.9\% in supervised learning, 5.2\% in reinforcement learning, and 5.1\% in test-time performance. These findings highlight the value of domain-specialized reward modeling for aligning LLMs with expert-level financial reasoning. Our project resources will be available at https://github.com/aliyun/qwen-dianjin.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Fin-PRM: A Domain-Specialized Process Reward Model for Financial Reasoning in Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Fin-PRM, a domain-specific Process Reward Model (PRM) tailored for evaluating intermediate reasoning steps in financial tasks performed by Large Language Models (LLMs). Unlike general-purpose PRMs, Fin-PRM integrates both step-level and trajectory-level reward supervision, enabling a more fine-grained and financially logical assessment of reasoning traces. The model is trained on a newly constructed dataset of 3,000 financial reasoning examples. The authors demonstrate Fin-PRM's effectiveness in three key applications: selecting high-quality reasoning trajectories for supervised fine-tuning, providing dense process-level rewards for reinforcement learning, and guiding reward-informed Best-of-N inference. Experimental results on financial reasoning benchmarks show that Fin-PRM outperforms general-purpose PRMs and strong domain baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty of this paper lies in the development of a *domain-specific* PRM for financial reasoning. Existing PRMs are often trained on more general STEM domains and lack the financial knowledge, regulatory awareness, and nuanced understanding required for tasks in the financial sector. The integration of *trajectory-aware* evaluation, combined with knowledge verification, and the dual-level training paradigm also demonstrates incremental novelty.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical need for improving the reliability and trustworthiness of LLMs in the high-stakes domain of finance. By providing a more accurate and interpretable reward signal, Fin-PRM helps to align LLM reasoning with expert-level financial analysis. The positive results across several different training/inference paradigms (SFT, RL, and Best-of-N) highlight the broad applicability and potential impact of the approach.
The creation of a financial dataset with reasoning traces and reward labels is a valuable contribution, especially given the scarcity of such resources in this domain.
The approach also enhances the *interpretability* of LLM-based financial reasoning, which is crucial for building user trust and ensuring regulatory compliance.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of general-purpose PRMs in the financial domain.
    *   **Comprehensive Approach:** Fin-PRM incorporates several key features, including step-level and trajectory-level rewards, knowledge verification, and a dual-level training paradigm.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experimental results demonstrate significant improvements over baseline methods across multiple tasks.
    *   **Well-Structured and Written:** The paper is well-organized and clearly explains the methodology and experimental results.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dataset Size:** While the 3,000-sample dataset is a valuable contribution, it's still relatively small for training a complex reward model, and the paper even acknowledges it. Scaling the dataset would likely further improve performance.
    *   **Static Knowledge Base:** The use of a static knowledge base is a potential limitation, as financial regulations and market conditions can change rapidly. Addressing this limitation would enhance the robustness and long-term applicability of the approach.
    *   **Computational Cost:** Fine-tuning a Qwen2.5-7B model and also querying LLMs such as Qwen3-235b-a22b for reward generation in a process that is knowledge verification intensive, suggests that the development may not be practical on devices with lower computational power.

*   **Potential Influence:**
    *   The paper is likely to stimulate further research in domain-specific reward modeling for LLMs.
    *   The Fin-PRM framework could be extended to other high-stakes domains, such as law and medicine.
    *   The dataset and methodology could serve as a valuable resource for training and evaluating LLMs for financial reasoning.

*   **Score Justification:** The paper makes a solid contribution by creating a domain-specific PRM for financial tasks. While the dataset size and static knowledge base represent limitations, the clear problem definition, comprehensive approach, and strong experimental results make this a worthwhile contribution. This is not a groundbreaking theoretical breakthrough, but it is a useful, well-executed engineering effort.

### R-ConstraintBench: Evaluating LLMs on NP-Complete Scheduling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15204v1)
- **Authors**: Raj Jain, Marc Wetter
- **Abstract**: Effective scheduling under tight resource, timing, and operational constraints underpins large-scale planning across sectors such as capital projects, manufacturing, logistics, and IT fleet transitions. However, the reliability of large language models (LLMs) when reasoning under high-constraint regimes is insufficiently characterized. To address this gap, we present R-ConstraintBench, a scalable framework that evaluates models on Resource-Constrained Project Scheduling Problems (RCPSP), an NP-Complete feasibility class, while difficulty increases via linear growth in constraints. R-ConstraintBench incrementally increases non-redundant precedence constraints in Directed Acyclic Graphs (DAGs) and then introduces downtime, temporal windows, and disjunctive constraints. As an illustrative example, we instantiate the benchmark in a data center migration setting and evaluate multiple LLMs using feasibility and error analysis, identifying degradation thresholds and constraint types most associated with failure. Empirically, strong models are near-ceiling on precedence-only DAGs, but feasibility performance collapses when downtime, temporal windows, and disjunctive constraints interact, implicating constraint interaction, not graph depth, as the principal bottleneck. Performance on clean synthetic ramps also does not guarantee transfer to domain-grounded scenarios, underscoring limited generalization.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "R-ConstraintBench: Evaluating LLMs on NP-Complete Scheduling":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces R-ConstraintBench, a benchmarking framework designed to evaluate the performance of Large Language Models (LLMs) on Resource-Constrained Project Scheduling Problems (RCPSP). The framework scales complexity by incrementally adding non-redundant precedence constraints, resource downtimes, temporal windows, and disjunctive constraints. The authors apply the benchmark to a data center migration scenario and assess the feasibility of LLM-generated schedules. The results highlight that while LLMs perform well on precedence-only constraints, their performance collapses when downtime, temporal windows, and disjunctive constraints interact, suggesting that constraint interaction, rather than graph depth, is the major bottleneck. Furthermore, the study finds that strong performance on clean synthetic benchmarks does not guarantee transfer to domain-grounded scenarios, indicating limited generalization.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper offers a valuable contribution by focusing on the often-overlooked area of LLM reliability under complex, real-world constraints. While LLMs have demonstrated proficiency in various domains, their capacity to handle intricately structured problems like RCPSP with many constraints has been relatively unexplored. The R-ConstraintBench framework systematically increases constraint complexity, providing a more controlled evaluation environment than simply throwing a complex problem at the LLM. The approach of incremental addition of different constraint types (precedence, temporal, resource, disjunctive) to isolate failure modes is novel and insightful.  However, the core concept of benchmarking LLMs on scheduling problems isn't entirely new. The novelty stems from the framework's design specifically focused on controlled complexity and the inclusion of varied constraint types.

**Significance:**

The paper's findings have important implications for deploying LLMs in real-world scheduling scenarios. The demonstration that performance on basic precedence constraints does not translate into robustness against more complex constraint interactions raises concerns about the reliability of these models in critical applications. The analysis of failure modes provides valuable insights into the specific challenges LLMs face, which can guide future research and development efforts.

The data center migration scenario provides a practical illustration of the limitations of current LLMs in complex scheduling problems. This use case helps bridge the gap between synthetic benchmarks and real-world applications, increasing the relevance and impact of the findings.

**Strengths:**

*   **Controlled Framework:** R-ConstraintBench provides a systematic and controlled environment for evaluating LLM performance.
*   **Focused Evaluation:** By focusing on feasibility rather than optimization, the authors isolate the core reasoning abilities of LLMs.
*   **Real-World Application:** The data center migration scenario demonstrates the practical relevance of the benchmark and the limitations of current LLMs.
*   **Failure Mode Analysis:** The classification of errors into different violation categories provides valuable insights into the challenges LLMs face.
*   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and organized, making it easy to understand the methodology and results.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Prompt Engineering:** The use of a single-shot prompt may limit the potential performance of the LLMs. Exploring multi-step prompting, chain-of-thought reasoning, or other prompt engineering techniques could improve results.
*   **Limited Domain Scope:** The focus on data center migration, while valuable, may not generalize to all types of scheduling problems. Evaluating LLMs on other domains, such as manufacturing or transportation logistics, would strengthen the conclusions.
*   **Solver Integration:** The paper mentions that specialized tool use is not permitted; however, integrating a solver to check the feasibility of intermediate steps or to assist in schedule refinement could improve overall performance and better represent realistic deployment scenarios.
*   **Model Selection:** While the authors evaluated a range of models, further analysis could compare models based on architecture (e.g. transformers vs. retrieval-augmented) and training methodology to analyze how these characteristics influence the models' performances.
*   **Generative Bias:**  Is the generator itself, despite efforts, introducing subtle biases?  This isn't explicitly addressed.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence the field by:

*   **Guiding future research:** The findings can guide researchers to focus on developing LLMs that are more robust to constraint interactions.
*   **Informing deployment strategies:** The results can help practitioners make informed decisions about deploying LLMs in real-world scheduling applications, taking into account the limitations of current models.
*   **Providing a benchmark:** R-ConstraintBench can serve as a standard benchmark for evaluating the performance of LLMs on RCPSP and related problems.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper is a solid contribution to the growing field of LLM evaluation. Its strength lies in the systematic framework and the focus on a practically relevant and complex scheduling problem. The failure mode analysis and emphasis on domain transferability are particularly valuable. While the limitations mentioned above slightly reduce the overall impact, the paper provides a significant contribution. The authors address an important gap in the existing literature, offer rigorous experimental results, and provide valuable insights.
