- **Score**: 7/10

### AmbiSQL: Interactive Ambiguity Detection and Resolution for Text-to-SQL
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15276v1)
- **Authors**: Zhongjun Ding, Yin Lin, Tianjing Zeng
- **Abstract**: Text-to-SQL systems translate natural language questions into SQL queries, providing substantial value for non-expert users. While large language models (LLMs) show promising results for this task, they remain error-prone. Query ambiguity has been recognized as a major obstacle for LLM-based Text-to-SQL systems, leading to misinterpretation of user intent and inaccurate SQL generation. We demonstrate AmbiSQL, an interactive system that automatically detects query ambiguities and guides users through intuitive multiple-choice questions to clarify their intent. Our approach introduces a fine-grained ambiguity taxonomy for identifying ambiguities that affect database element mapping and LLM reasoning, then incorporates user feedback to rewrite ambiguous questions. Evaluation on an ambiguous query dataset shows that AmbiSQL achieves 87.2% precision in ambiguity detection and improves SQL exact match accuracy by 50% when integrated with Text-to-SQL systems. Our demonstration showcases the significant performance gains and highlights the system's practical usability. Code repo and demonstration are available at: https://github.com/JustinzjDing/AmbiSQL.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the AmbiSQL paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AmbiSQL, an interactive system designed to improve the accuracy of Text-to-SQL translation by addressing the problem of query ambiguity.  AmbiSQL automatically detects ambiguities in natural language questions using in-context learning with a fine-grained ambiguity taxonomy. It then guides users through a series of multiple-choice clarification questions to resolve these ambiguities before generating a SQL query.  The system integrates as a preprocessing module for existing Text-to-SQL systems.  Evaluation on an ambiguous query dataset shows that AmbiSQL achieves high precision in ambiguity detection and significantly improves SQL exact match accuracy compared to the baseline Text-to-SQL system. The authors demonstrate the usability and performance gains of AmbiSQL through a user-friendly interface.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The novelty of AmbiSQL lies primarily in its comprehensive ambiguity taxonomy and its seamless integration into existing Text-to-SQL pipelines as a pre-processing step. While interactive ambiguity resolution isn't entirely new, this paper provides a detailed taxonomy of ambiguity types affecting both database element mapping and LLM reasoning, which is a valuable contribution. The use of in-context learning for ambiguity detection and classification is a practical approach given the limitations of training data for specific ambiguity types.  The multi-choice clarification question format also simplifies the user experience.
* **Significance:**  The significance of AmbiSQL stems from its ability to significantly improve the accuracy of Text-to-SQL systems in the presence of ambiguity.  This is a critical problem that has been identified as a major source of errors in current LLM-based Text-to-SQL models. The demonstration that integrating AmbiSQL as a pre-processing step can boost exact match accuracy by up to 50% on ambiguous queries is quite substantial. Furthermore, it helps bridge the gap between the capabilities of Text-to-SQL systems and real-world user queries, which are often inherently ambiguous.  The focus on LLM reasoning ambiguity is timely, as these kinds of complex queries become more common and critical.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Comprehensive Ambiguity Taxonomy:**  The paper provides a well-defined and useful classification of ambiguity types, covering both database-related and LLM-reasoning related sources.
    * **Practical Approach:**  The use of in-context learning and multiple-choice questions makes the system practical and easy to use. It avoids the need for large labeled datasets for ambiguity detection and classification, and simplifies user interaction.
    * **Significant Performance Gains:**  The experimental results clearly demonstrate the effectiveness of AmbiSQL in improving the accuracy of Text-to-SQL systems on ambiguous queries.
    * **User-Friendly Interface:**  The demonstration showcases a well-designed and intuitive user interface that guides users through the ambiguity resolution process.
    * **Reproducibility:** The authors have provided code and demo, which enhances the reproducability and further research on it.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Dataset:** The evaluation dataset, while covering the ambiguity categories, could be larger to demonstrate more robust generalizability.  A more comprehensive comparison to other ambiguity resolution techniques could also be beneficial.
    * **Dependency on LLMs:** While the use of LLMs is advantageous, the system's performance is inherently tied to the capabilities of the chosen LLM (GPT-40 in this case).  Changes or limitations in the LLM could affect AmbiSQL's performance.
    * **Potential for User Fatigue:**  While multiple-choice questions are easier, long sequences of clarification questions could lead to user fatigue, especially for highly ambiguous queries. The paper doesn't address ways to minimize the number of required questions.

* **Potential Influence:** AmbiSQL has the potential to influence the field of Text-to-SQL by:
    * Providing a framework for systematically addressing query ambiguity.
    * Inspiring the development of more interactive and user-centric Text-to-SQL systems.
    * Enabling the creation of more robust and reliable Text-to-SQL models that can handle real-world queries.

**Overall:**

AmbiSQL represents a valuable contribution to the field of Text-to-SQL. It offers a practical and effective solution for addressing the challenging problem of query ambiguity, and is shown to deliver substantial accuracy improvements. The detailed ambiguity taxonomy and user-friendly interface are also notable strengths. While there are some weaknesses related to dataset size and reliance on LLMs, the overall quality and significance of the work are high.

Score: 8. The paper provides significant improvements in addressing query ambiguity in Text-to-SQL, is well-executed, but is not completely groundbreaking. The reliance on an LLM prevents it from a higher score, as future improvements to LLMs will be needed to further enhance AmbiSQL's performance.

- **Classification**: cs.DB
- **Score**: 8/10

### Adversarial Attacks against Neural Ranking Models via In-Context Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15283v1)
- **Authors**: Amin Bigdeli, Negar Arabzadeh, Ebrahim Bagheri, Charles L. A. Clarke
- **Abstract**: While neural ranking models (NRMs) have shown high effectiveness, they remain susceptible to adversarial manipulation. In this work, we introduce Few-Shot Adversarial Prompting (FSAP), a novel black-box attack framework that leverages the in-context learning capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) to generate high-ranking adversarial documents. Unlike previous approaches that rely on token-level perturbations or manual rewriting of existing documents, FSAP formulates adversarial attacks entirely through few-shot prompting, requiring no gradient access or internal model instrumentation. By conditioning the LLM on a small support set of previously observed harmful examples, FSAP synthesizes grammatically fluent and topically coherent documents that subtly embed false or misleading information and rank competitively against authentic content. We instantiate FSAP in two modes: FSAP-IntraQ, which leverages harmful examples from the same query to enhance topic fidelity, and FSAP-InterQ, which enables broader generalization by transferring adversarial patterns across unrelated queries. Our experiments on the TREC 2020 and 2021 Health Misinformation Tracks, using four diverse neural ranking models, reveal that FSAP-generated documents consistently outrank credible, factually accurate documents. Furthermore, our analysis demonstrates that these adversarial outputs exhibit strong stance alignment and low detectability, posing a realistic and scalable threat to neural retrieval systems. FSAP also effectively generalizes across both proprietary and open-source LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Few-Shot Adversarial Prompting (FSAP), a novel black-box attack framework targeting Neural Ranking Models (NRMs). FSAP leverages the in-context learning capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) to generate high-ranking adversarial documents without requiring gradient access or internal model instrumentation.  It works by conditioning the LLM on a small set of previously observed harmful examples. Two modes are proposed: FSAPIntraQ, which uses examples from the same query, and FSAPInterQ, which transfers patterns across unrelated queries. Experiments on TREC 2020 and 2021 Health Misinformation Tracks, using diverse NRMs, demonstrate that FSAP-generated documents outrank credible ones, exhibiting strong stance alignment and low detectability.  The approach generalizes across both proprietary and open-source LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*Novelty:*

The core novelty of the paper lies in its innovative application of few-shot prompting to autonomously generate adversarial documents specifically designed to manipulate NRMs. While previous work has explored LLM-based misinformation generation and adversarial attacks in IR, the combination of in-context learning with a focus on bypassing detection mechanisms in the context of ranking is a unique contribution.  The distinction between FSAPIntraQ and FSAPInterQ, offering different trade-offs between topic fidelity and generalizability, is also a valuable aspect.  It improves on prior studies that focused on altering existing documents or hallucinating content with less control over the ranking outcome.  The use of an indicator loss function to formalize the goal of having the adversarial document outrank a regular one, further adds to the technical depth.

*Significance:*

The findings have significant implications for the security and reliability of information retrieval systems, particularly in sensitive domains like health. The paper highlights a realistic and scalable threat posed by LLMs, which can generate persuasive and difficult-to-detect deceptive content. The demonstration that LLMs can be exploited to *induce* the generation of content that deceives ranking algorithms without any fine tuning is important. It raises concerns about the potential for large-scale dissemination of misinformation. The use of readily available datasets and models increases the reproducibility and relevance of the work.

*Strengths:*

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly defines the adversarial problem and motivates its importance.
*   **Well-Defined Methodology:** FSAP is well-defined, and the two modes (IntraQ and InterQ) are clearly explained.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The experiments are extensive, using multiple datasets, NRMs, and LLMs.  The use of MHDR, stance alignment, and detection pass metrics provides a multi-faceted evaluation.
*   **Strong Results:** The results convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of FSAP in generating adversarial documents that outrank credible content.
*   **Analysis of Trade-offs:** The paper analyzes the trade-offs between attack performance, stance alignment, and detectability.
*   **Generalizability:** The evaluation of both proprietary (GPT-40) and open-source (DeepSeek) LLMs, demonstrates the applicability of the approach across different platforms.
*   **Public Availability:** The code and data being publicly available contribute to reproducibility and future research.

*Weaknesses:*

*   **Reliance on GPT-4 and DeepSeek:** The experiments primarily rely on GPT-4 (API based) and DeepSeek R1-claude3.7. While justified by their performance, exploring other LLMs or even different versions of the same models could strengthen the generalizability claims.  The limitations of running FSAP IntraQ on all topics due to data restrictions could also be highlighted more.
*   **Zero-Shot Stance Detection Limitation**: Using GPT-4 to zero-shot stance detection has limitations in terms of consistency and might introduce biases.
*   **Simplistic threat Model:** The proposed approach focuses on single stage attack. In a real-world scenario, adversaries may be able to take intermediate steps that were ignored by the current model. For example, to fine-tune the LLMs before using for generating the final adversarial documents.

*Potential Influence:*

The paper is likely to influence future research in several directions:

*   **Adversarial Robustness of NRMs:** It will motivate further research on developing more robust NRMs that are resistant to LLM-generated adversarial attacks.
*   **Content Moderation and Detection:** It will spur development of more sophisticated content moderation techniques that can detect and filter deceptive content generated by LLMs.
*   **LLM Security:** It will contribute to a broader understanding of the security risks associated with LLMs and how they can be exploited for malicious purposes.
*   **Few-Shot Learning for Security:**  It may inspire new applications of few-shot learning for other security-related tasks.

**Score: 8**

*Rationale:*

The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to generating adversarial documents that can effectively manipulate NRMs. The findings are significant, demonstrating a real threat to information retrieval systems. The comprehensive evaluation, clear presentation, and public availability of code and data contribute to the paper's value. The limitations regarding the dependence on certain LLMs and simplistic threat model do detract somewhat from the score, however, the overall contribution is significant. The novelty and significant findings, along with the solid methodology and experimentation, justify a score of 8. The work lays a strong foundation for future research on adversarial robustness and content moderation in the age of LLMs.

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 8/10

### Multiple Memory Systems for Enhancing the Long-term Memory of Agent
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15294v1)
- **Authors**: Gaoke Zhang, Bo Wang, Yunlong Ma, Dongming Zhao, Zifei Yu
- **Abstract**: An agent powered by large language models have achieved impressive results, but effectively handling the vast amounts of historical data generated during interactions remains a challenge. The current approach is to design a memory module for the agent to process these data. However, existing methods, such as MemoryBank and A-MEM, have poor quality of stored memory content, which affects recall performance and response quality. In order to better construct high-quality long-term memory content, we have designed a multiple memory system (MMS) inspired by cognitive psychology theory. The system processes short-term memory to multiple long-term memory fragments, and constructs retrieval memory units and contextual memory units based on these fragments, with a one-to-one correspondence between the two. During the retrieval phase, MMS will match the most relevant retrieval memory units based on the user's query. Then, the corresponding contextual memory units is obtained as the context for the response stage to enhance knowledge, thereby effectively utilizing historical data. Experiments on LoCoMo dataset compared our method with three others, proving its effectiveness. Ablation studies confirmed the rationality of our memory units. We also analyzed the robustness regarding the number of selected memory segments and the storage overhead, demonstrating its practical value.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a "Multiple Memory System" (MMS) designed to enhance the long-term memory capabilities of LLM-based agents.  The core idea is to improve the *quality* of stored memory content.  Inspired by cognitive psychology principles like Tulving's memory system classification and the encoding specificity principle, MMS processes short-term memory into multiple long-term memory fragments (keywords, cognitive perspectives, episodic memory, and semantic memory).  It then constructs "retrieval memory units" for relevance matching and "contextual memory units" to provide enhanced knowledge during response generation.  Experiments on the LoCoMo dataset demonstrate that MMS outperforms existing methods like Naive RAG, MemoryBank, and A-MEM in both retrieval and generation tasks. Ablation studies confirm the importance of the different memory fragments.  The paper also analyzes the robustness of the method and its storage overhead.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**

    * **Addresses a Relevant Problem:**  The paper tackles a critical limitation of LLM agents: effectively managing and utilizing long-term memory.  The focus on improving the *quality* of stored memory is a valuable contribution, contrasting with methods that primarily focus on storage mechanisms or retrieval efficiency.
    * **Solid Theoretical Grounding:**  Drawing inspiration from cognitive psychology provides a strong foundation for the proposed memory architecture.  The application of Tulving's concepts and encoding specificity is well-reasoned and helps justify the design choices.
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The experimental setup is thorough, using a well-established dataset (LoCoMo) and standard evaluation metrics.  The comparison against multiple baselines provides a good benchmark for the MMS's performance. Ablation studies offer further insights into the contribution of each memory fragment. Analysis on number of memories and memory overhead provides valuable practical consideration for real-world deployment.
    * **Clear and Well-Written:** The paper is generally well-structured and easy to follow.  The explanations of the MMS components and the experimental results are clear and concise. The inclusion of Figure 1 to illustrate the system process helps in grasping the concept.
* **Weaknesses:**

    * **Incremental Novelty:**  While the combination of ideas is sound, the individual components are not entirely novel.  The use of keywords, summaries, and different perspectives in memory systems has been explored in previous works. The novelty primarily lies in the specific combination of these elements within the MMS architecture and its grounding in cognitive psychology principles.
    * **LLM Dependency and Complexity:**  The reliance on LLMs for short-term memory processing adds complexity and introduces potential limitations related to LLM biases, costs, and scalability.  The paper could benefit from a more in-depth discussion of these challenges.
    * **Lack of Detail in LLM Prompts/Configurations:** The experimental details lack specifics on the LLM prompts used to generate memory fragments and responses. Providing these prompts would enhance reproducibility and allow for a more detailed understanding of the method's performance.
    * **Scalability Considerations:** While the paper analyzes storage overhead, it doesn't fully address the scalability of MMS in terms of handling extremely large and complex dialogue histories. The latency introduced by the additional processing steps in MMS might become a bottleneck in real-time applications.

* **Significance:**

    The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of LLM agents by highlighting the importance of memory *quality* and offering a theoretically grounded approach to improve it.  The MMS architecture has the potential to enhance the reasoning, consistency, and personalization capabilities of dialogue systems. While the novelty is incremental, the solid evaluation and practical considerations make it a worthwhile contribution. The work will likely influence future research on memory systems for LLMs.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:**

The paper presents a reasonably novel memory system (MMS) for LLM agents that demonstrates performance improvements over existing methods. The MMS is grounded in cognitive psychology principles and is comprehensively evaluated on a standard dataset, thereby increasing the credibility and significance of the results. The key strength of this paper is the focus on memory content *quality* rather than simply storage and retrieval. However, the components of the MMS are not entirely novel, and the paper's reliance on LLMs might introduce scalability issues. Furthermore, additional details on the LLM prompt engineering aspect would aid in reproducibility. Overall, the paper makes a solid contribution, but could have provided a more detailed discussion of LLM-related limitations and address potential scalability issues. A 7 reflects a contribution that is valuable and influential, but not groundbreaking or transformative.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### MLLMRec: Exploring the Potential of Multimodal Large Language Models in Recommender Systems
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15304v1)
- **Authors**: Yuzhuo Dang, Xin Zhang, Zhiqiang Pan, Yuxiao Duan, Wanyu Chen, Fei Cai, Honghui Chen
- **Abstract**: Multimodal recommendation typically combines the user behavioral data with the modal features of items to reveal user's preference, presenting superior performance compared to the conventional recommendations. However, existing methods still suffer from two key problems: (1) the initialization methods of user multimodal representations are either behavior-unperceived or noise-contaminated, and (2) the KNN-based item-item graph contains noisy edges with low similarities and lacks audience co-occurrence relationships. To address such issues, we propose MLLMRec, a novel MLLM-driven multimodal recommendation framework with two item-item graph refinement strategies. On the one hand, the item images are first converted into high-quality semantic descriptions using an MLLM, which are then fused with the textual metadata of items. Then, we construct a behavioral description list for each user and feed it into the MLLM to reason about the purified user preference containing interaction motivations. On the other hand, we design the threshold-controlled denoising and topology-aware enhancement strategies to refine the suboptimal item-item graph, thereby enhancing the item representation learning. Extensive experiments on three publicly available datasets demonstrate that MLLMRec achieves the state-of-the-art performance with an average improvement of 38.53% over the best baselines.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MLLMRec, a novel framework for multimodal recommendation systems leveraging Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). It addresses two key limitations in existing MMRS methods: inaccurate initialization of user representations and noisy edges in item-item graphs. MLLMRec uses the MLLM to generate semantic descriptions of item images, which are then combined with textual metadata. A user's interaction history is then converted into a natural language description to guide the MLLM in reasoning about user preferences. Furthermore, it employs threshold-controlled denoising and topology-aware enhancement to refine the item-item graph. Experiments on three datasets demonstrate state-of-the-art performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper exhibits a reasonable degree of novelty by incorporating MLLMs in several aspects of the recommendation process. Converting images to text descriptions using an MLLM, using the MLLM for user preference reasoning based on interaction history, and the combined denoising and topology-aware graph refinement strategy are distinct contributions. The user preference reasoning using LLMs is particularly noteworthy, as it moves beyond simply embedding or augmenting existing representations.

**Significance:** The paper tackles the important problem of improving the quality of user and item representations in multimodal recommendation. By addressing the issues of noisy user initializations and suboptimal item graphs, MLLMRec leads to improved performance. Experimental results suggest the framework's effectiveness, and the plug-and-play nature of the graph refinement component adds to its practical significance. The reported performance gains, particularly the average improvement of 38.53% over baselines, are significant.

**Strengths:**

*   **Integration of MLLMs:** The paper effectively demonstrates how MLLMs can be utilized for semantic description generation and user preference reasoning in a multimodal setting.
*   **Comprehensive Approach:**  The paper addresses both user and item representation quality, making the approach more complete.
*   **Graph Refinement:** The proposed graph refinement techniques are sensible and provide an improvement over the standard KNN method.
*   **Strong Experimental Results:** The experimental evaluation is thorough, employing a range of baselines and ablation studies to validate the contributions of each component. The gains over strong baselines on multiple datasets are compelling.
*   **Ablation Studies:** Provide valuable insights into the importance of each component of the model.
*   **Plug-and-Play nature:** Offers a practical component that can be applied to existing models.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Computational Cost:** The use of MLLMs likely introduces a significant computational overhead compared to traditional methods. The paper should address or mitigate this concern, perhaps by discussing optimization techniques or model compression strategies. It needs to be specified how much time it takes to generate descriptions for items/users during the training and testing phase.
*   **Generalizability:** The experiments are conducted on Amazon datasets. While these are standard benchmarks, evaluating the framework on more diverse and real-world datasets would further strengthen the claims of generalizability. The datasets considered may have specific biases, that could affect the performance of the proposed method, as also indicated in the discussion of RQ4.
*   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The sensitivity analysis of alpha and Ke indicates the model may be sensitive to hyperparameter tuning. This could make the model more difficult to deploy and use in practice.
*   **MLLM Selection:** A more detailed justification of the choice of the Gemma3-27b model is necessary, particularly considering the variety of open-source and proprietary MLLMs available. Why this model was selected and how it compares to other models in terms of performance and computational cost needs to be better justified.
*   **Limited User-Item Graph Use:** MLLMRec avoids the user-item graph convolution, which is usually key for learning high-order relations. The justification for this seems to be "removing it makes the system work better due to homogenization" but further analysis and explanation is needed as to why these relations degrade the performance.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence the field of multimodal recommendation by demonstrating how MLLMs can be effectively incorporated to improve representation learning. The graph refinement strategies also offer a valuable contribution that can be adopted in other models. The focus on refining initial user representation with MLLM and its strong results provide other researchers a direction to look into, potentially leading to improvements in the current methodologies in multimodal recommendation systems.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:**

The paper demonstrates good novelty and significance. The approach of using MLLMs for semantic description generation and user preference reasoning is innovative and addresses the existing limitations of MMRS models. The plug-and-play nature of the refinement strategies provides a valuable contribution and opens the door to future works. However, the relatively high computational cost and high reliance on hyperparameters, and lack of detail on some methodological choices, limit its score. While the performance improvements are impressive, a more in-depth analysis of the trade-offs between accuracy and efficiency, as well as more thorough benchmark testing, would further strengthen the paper. Its strong performance and interesting techniques justify a score of 7.5, however.

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Coarse-to-Fine Grounded Memory for LLM Agent Planning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15305v1)
- **Authors**: Wei Yang, Jinwei Xiao, Hongming Zhang, Qingyang Zhang, Yanna Wang, Bo Xu
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in Large Language Models (LLMs) have driven growing interest in LLM-based agents for complex planning tasks. To avoid costly agent training, many studies adopted memory mechanism that enhances LLM with offline experiences or online trajectory analysis. However, existing works focus on single-granularity memory derived from dynamic environmental interactions, which are inherently constrained by the quality of the collected experiences. This limitation, in turn, constrain the diversity of knowledge and the flexibility of planning. We propose Coarse-to-Fine Grounded Memory (\Ours{}), a novel framework that grounds coarse-to-fine memories with LLM, thereby fully leverage them for flexible adaptation to diverse scenarios. \Ours{} grounds environmental information into coarse-grained focus points to guide experience collection in training tasks, followed by grounding of actionable hybrid-grained tips from each experience. At inference, \Ours{} retrieves task-relevant experiences and tips to support planning. When facing environmental anomalies, the LLM grounds the current situation into fine-grained key information, enabling flexible self-QA reflection and plan correction.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Coarse-to-Fine Grounded Memory (CFGM), a novel framework designed to improve the planning capabilities of Large Language Model (LLM) agents in complex interactive environments. CFGM addresses the limitations of existing memory mechanisms that rely on single-granularity, experience-derived data by grounding coarse-to-fine memories with LLM's internal knowledge.  The approach involves three stages: (1) identifying coarse-grained focus points to guide initial exploration, (2) extracting hybrid-grained tips from experiences, and (3) dynamically adapting plans using fine-grained key information during online interactions (Self-QA reflection).  Experiments on AlfWorld, Webshop, and ScienceWorld demonstrate that CFGM significantly outperforms existing memory-enhanced LLM agents.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a compelling and well-structured approach to memory augmentation in LLM agents. The core novelty lies in the hierarchical, coarse-to-fine grounding of memories using LLM's own knowledge.  This differs significantly from existing methods that often rely on external data, hand-engineered prompts, or single-granularity representations. The integration of focus point-driven experience collection is innovative, as is the hybrid-grained tip extraction.

*   **Significance:**  The results convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of CFGM in enhancing the performance and robustness of LLM agents across diverse and challenging interactive environments. The performance gains are substantial (up to +31% on ScienceWorld), and the ablation studies provide evidence that each component of the framework contributes to the overall improvement. By addressing the limitations of inefficient exploration, knowledge homogenization, and lack of error-correction flexibility, CFGM offers a significant advancement in the field of LLM agents. Also, It demonstrated excellent transferability using the transfer learning experiment on WebArena-Shopping

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined problem and clear solution:**  The paper clearly articulates the challenges with existing memory mechanisms and provides a well-defined solution (CFGM) that addresses those challenges.
    *   **Comprehensive experimental evaluation:** The authors conduct extensive experiments on three diverse benchmarks, demonstrating the effectiveness and generalizability of CFGM.
    *   **Detailed ablation studies:**  The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of each component of the framework, validating the design choices.
    *   **Strong performance gains:** CFGM achieves significant performance improvements over strong baselines, showcasing the potential of the approach.
    *   **Well-written and organized:** The paper is well-written, clearly explains the methodology, and presents the results in a concise and organized manner.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational Cost:** The paper doesn't delve into the computational overhead associated with the additional LLM calls for focus point generation, tip extraction, and key information reflection. A discussion on the efficiency and scalability of CFGM would strengthen the paper.
    *   **Model Dependency:** Although the authors test CFGM with multiple models and demonstrate its effectiveness, the reliance on high-performing LLMs (GPT-4) for certain components (knowledge extraction) could be a limitation. The sensitivity of CFGM's performance to the specific LLM used should be further investigated.

*   **Potential Influence:** The CFGM framework has the potential to significantly influence the design of future memory-enhanced LLM agents.  The coarse-to-fine grounding approach can be adapted to various tasks and environments. The methodology for integrating LLM internal knowledge with external memories is a valuable contribution. The demonstration of strong generalizability across different models enhances its applicability.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper demonstrates a novel and significant contribution to the field of LLM agents. CFGM tackles critical limitations of existing memory mechanisms through a well-designed and rigorously evaluated framework. It achieves substantial performance improvements across multiple benchmarks and is expected to influence future research directions. While the computational cost and potential model dependency are factors to consider, the overall contribution is significant.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 8/10

### VideoEraser: Concept Erasure in Text-to-Video Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15314v1)
- **Authors**: Naen Xu, Jinghuai Zhang, Changjiang Li, Zhi Chen, Chunyi Zhou, Qingming Li, Tianyu Du, Shouling Ji
- **Abstract**: The rapid growth of text-to-video (T2V) diffusion models has raised concerns about privacy, copyright, and safety due to their potential misuse in generating harmful or misleading content. These models are often trained on numerous datasets, including unauthorized personal identities, artistic creations, and harmful materials, which can lead to uncontrolled production and distribution of such content. To address this, we propose VideoEraser, a training-free framework that prevents T2V diffusion models from generating videos with undesirable concepts, even when explicitly prompted with those concepts. Designed as a plug-and-play module, VideoEraser can seamlessly integrate with representative T2V diffusion models via a two-stage process: Selective Prompt Embedding Adjustment (SPEA) and Adversarial-Resilient Noise Guidance (ARNG). We conduct extensive evaluations across four tasks, including object erasure, artistic style erasure, celebrity erasure, and explicit content erasure. Experimental results show that VideoEraser consistently outperforms prior methods regarding efficacy, integrity, fidelity, robustness, and generalizability. Notably, VideoEraser achieves state-of-the-art performance in suppressing undesirable content during T2V generation, reducing it by 46% on average across four tasks compared to baselines.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "VideoEraser: Concept Erasure in Text-to-Video Diffusion Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces VideoEraser, a training-free framework designed to prevent text-to-video (T2V) diffusion models from generating videos containing undesirable concepts.  The framework addresses concerns related to privacy, copyright, and safety arising from the potential misuse of T2V models. VideoEraser works as a plug-and-play module and operates in two stages: Selective Prompt Embedding Adjustment (SPEA), which adjusts text embeddings to suppress target concepts, and Adversarial-Resilient Noise Guidance (ARNG), which steers latent noise away from the undesirable concept while maintaining consistency. The authors evaluate VideoEraser across object erasure, artistic style erasure, celebrity erasure, and explicit content erasure tasks.  The results show VideoEraser consistently outperforms existing methods in terms of efficacy, integrity, fidelity, robustness, and generalizability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a novel two-stage framework (SPEA and ARNG) that is training-free and plug-and-play. While existing concept erasure methods exist for T2I models and some for T2V, the specific combination of SPEA and ARNG, tailored to the unique challenges of T2V, does introduce a degree of novelty. The attention to adversarial robustness in the design is also a noteworthy aspect. SPEA itself leverages previous token sensitivity analysis approaches, but adapts it for the text-to-video domain with a modified method. The claim of novelty in its approach of adversarial robustness should be highlighted as existing T2V concept erasure methods tend to focus on text encoders.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial problem in the responsible development and deployment of T2V models.  The potential for misuse of these models to generate harmful or infringing content is a significant barrier to their wider adoption. The proposed framework could enable safer T2V generation. While retraining is often impractical, model operators now have a straightforward concept erasure solution that integrates into common T2V workflows, and has been empirically tested across four different target concept domains. Given its integration capability and performance, this approach serves as an important step in mitigating the risk of the harmful creation that could be generated from T2V models.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Training-free:** Being training-free is a huge advantage, making it practically applicable without significant computational overhead.
    *   **Plug-and-play:** Its ease of integration is a significant strength.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The thorough evaluation across multiple tasks and metrics strengthens the validity of the claims. The authors take a holistic approach, not just focusing on erasure efficacy, but also integrity, fidelity, robustness, and generalizability.
    *   **Adversarial Robustness:** Explicitly addressing and demonstrating robustness against adversarial attacks is a strong selling point.
    *   **Generalizability:** It would be great to explore if the technique can be generalized to other modalities besides video.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Computational Overhead:** While training-free, the approach does introduce some computational overhead during inference (1.4x slowdown), which might be a consideration for some applications. The claim that "both the optimized vanilla classifier guidance and our method require the same time during inference" needs to be further justified since the previous statement suggests that is not the case.
    *   **Abstract Concepts:** The limitations regarding abstract concepts (as mentioned by the authors) need to be considered. Is the proposed technique less effective with target concepts that are too general?
    *   **Metric limitations** There needs to be some discussion around the metric limitations when trying to determine if T2V is actually generating images. ResNet and Open AI are fine for helping in specific target concepts, but they may not give a perfect evaluation in other scenarios.
    *   **Some Visual results are not completely gone** Although videoeaser reduces the ACC by 46%, there are visual results where a trained eye can still visualize the concepts that were removed.
    *   **Ethics limitations** While the authors discuss that they follow all appropriate ethical considerations, there is a chance that these processes can be used to remove concepts that might be otherwise valuable or even lead to censorship. The techniques can only be used in an ethical scenario if there are appropriate use cases.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the development and deployment of T2V models by providing a practical and effective mechanism for concept erasure.  It could also spark further research into more efficient and robust concept erasure techniques.

*   **Overall Assessment:** The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of responsible AI by addressing a critical safety concern in T2V models. The training-free, plug-and-play nature of VideoEraser and its demonstrated robustness make it a practically useful tool. There are still some weaknesses but there are clear pathways for further research and optimization.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:** The paper presents a novel and practical solution to a significant problem in the field of T2V models. The experimental results are compelling, and the comprehensive evaluation strengthens the claims. While it's not a groundbreaking theoretical advancement, the practical utility and potential impact on the responsible development of T2V systems justify a high score. The limitations regarding overhead, the effectiveness in very abstract concepts, and that there are potentially different ways the removal is presented hinder it from achieving a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### RETAIL: Towards Real-world Travel Planning for Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15335v1)
- **Authors**: Bin Deng, Yizhe Feng, Zeming Liu, Qing Wei, Xiangrong Zhu, Shuai Chen, Yuanfang Guo, Yunhong Wang
- **Abstract**: Although large language models have enhanced automated travel planning abilities, current systems remain misaligned with real-world scenarios. First, they assume users provide explicit queries, while in reality requirements are often implicit. Second, existing solutions ignore diverse environmental factors and user preferences, limiting the feasibility of plans. Third, systems can only generate plans with basic POI arrangements, failing to provide all-in-one plans with rich details. To mitigate these challenges, we construct a novel dataset \textbf{RETAIL}, which supports decision-making for implicit queries while covering explicit queries, both with and without revision needs. It also enables environmental awareness to ensure plan feasibility under real-world scenarios, while incorporating detailed POI information for all-in-one travel plans. Furthermore, we propose a topic-guided multi-agent framework, termed TGMA. Our experiments reveal that even the strongest existing model achieves merely a 1.0% pass rate, indicating real-world travel planning remains extremely challenging. In contrast, TGMA demonstrates substantially improved performance 2.72%, offering promising directions for real-world travel planning.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RETAIL, a new dataset aimed at improving the real-world applicability of travel planning systems powered by large language models (LLMs).  The authors identify three key limitations in existing systems: reliance on explicit user queries, ignoring environmental factors and user needs, and generating basic plans lacking rich details (e.g., timing, tickets, UGC-enriched info).  To address these, RETAIL includes:
1.  **Decision-making support:** Facilitates implicit query handling through dialogue-based clarification.
2.  **Environmental awareness:** Considers environmental factors for feasibility and dynamic plan adjustment.
3.  **All-in-one plans:** Incorporates detailed POI information for comprehensive travel itineraries.
The paper also presents TGMA, a topic-guided multi-agent framework designed to leverage RETAIL for improved travel plan generation.  Experimental results demonstrate that TGMA significantly outperforms existing models in terms of pass rate, suggesting promise for real-world travel planning applications.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the construction of the RETAIL dataset. Existing travel planning datasets often focus on explicit queries or lack sufficient detail for practical applications. The inclusion of implicit query support, environmental awareness, and rich POI information represents a valuable contribution. The TGMA framework, while potentially effective, is less novel, as it builds upon existing LLM-based agent architectures and topic modeling.

*   **Significance:** The identified limitations in existing travel planning systems are well-articulated and represent significant barriers to real-world deployment. By providing a more realistic dataset and a corresponding framework, the paper addresses a crucial gap in the field. The experimental results, while limited to pass rate, demonstrate a substantial improvement over existing methods, suggesting the potential for TGMA and RETAIL to facilitate more practical and user-friendly travel planning systems.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Dataset:** The RETAIL dataset is a valuable resource, enabling research on more complex and realistic travel planning scenarios. The authors detail the dataset construction and annotation processes, which contributes to its reliability.
    *   **Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies and motivates the three key limitations in current travel planning systems.
    *   **Multi-Agent Framework:** The framework addresses the complexities of travel planning, by decomposing them into manageable tasks handled by specialized agents.
    *   **Experimental Results:** The significant improvement in pass rate achieved by TGMA compared to the baselines, demonstrates the viability of the approach for real-world planning.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Evaluation Metrics:** The primary evaluation metric is the pass rate of travel plans, which measures the overall quality. However, further analysis in the paper, especially, in multi-modal content/information, or in using more detailed travel parameters, could significantly increase the score.
    *   **Generalizability:** The RETAIL dataset is based on Chinese travel destinations. Generalizing the approach to other cultures and regions would require further investigation and potential adaptation.
    *   **Framework Novelty:** The TGMA framework relies on existing LLM agent framework paradigms.
    *   **Framework Ablation study:** More detail is needed about the inner functionality that produces higher scores, especially in high performance configurations, where the topic agent, or multi-agent structures, cause such scores.

*   **Potential Influence:** The RETAIL dataset is likely to stimulate further research on real-world travel planning systems. The TGMA framework could serve as a baseline for future development, and the modular design facilitates further improvements.

*   **Justification:** The strengths of the paper, centered around the realistic and detailed RETAIL dataset and the clearly articulated problem definition, combined with the significant, though narrowly measured, performance improvements demonstrated by TGMA, support a favorable assessment. However, the reliance on existing LLM framework architectures and the limited scope of the current evaluation prevent a higher score.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### DiagECG: An LLM-Driven Framework for Diagnostic Reasoning via Discretized ECG Tokenization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15338v1)
- **Authors**: Jinning Yang, Wen Shi
- **Abstract**: Electrocardiography plays a central role in cardiovascular diagnostics, yet existing automated approaches often struggle to generalize across clinical tasks and offer limited support for open-ended reasoning. We present DiagECG, a novel framework that integrates time-series and language modeling by enabling large language models to process 12-lead ECG signals for clinical text generation tasks. Our approach discretizes continuous ECG embeddings into symbolic tokens using a lead-independent encoder and quantization module. These tokens are then used to extend the vocabulary of LLM, allowing the model to handle both ECG and natural language inputs in a unified manner. To bridge the modality gap, we pretrain the model on an autoregressive ECG forecasting task, enabling the LLM to model temporal dynamics using its native language modeling capabilities. Finally, we perform instruction tuning on both ECG question answering and diagnostic report generation. Without modifying the core model, DiagECG achieves strong performance across tasks while maintaining generalization to out-of-distribution settings. Extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of each component and highlight the potential of integrating symbolic ECG representations into LLMs for medical reasoning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the DiagECG paper:

**Summary:**

The DiagECG paper introduces a novel framework for integrating time-series ECG data with large language models (LLMs) to enable diagnostic reasoning. The key innovation lies in discretizing continuous ECG embeddings into symbolic tokens using a lead-independent encoder and quantization module. These tokens are then used to extend the LLM's vocabulary, allowing it to process ECG data and natural language in a unified manner. The framework pretrains the model on an autoregressive ECG forecasting task and performs instruction tuning on ECG question answering and diagnostic report generation. DiagECG achieves strong performance across various tasks and demonstrates good generalization to out-of-distribution settings without modifying the core LLM architecture.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates decent novelty in its approach to bridging the gap between continuous physiological signals (ECGs) and discrete language models. Converting ECG signals into symbolic tokens that can be directly incorporated into an LLM's vocabulary is a key distinguishing factor from previous methods that rely on continuous embeddings and complex cross-modal alignment techniques. The lead-wise encoder followed by fixed-scale quantization allows LLMs to perform more effectively on ECG data.  The autoregressive pretraining approach is also a relatively simple but effective method for capturing ECG temporal information.

* **Significance:** The significance lies in the potential to improve ECG interpretation and diagnostic accuracy by leveraging the reasoning capabilities of LLMs. The proposed framework tackles challenges such as modality gaps between ECG signals and textual semantics. It also addresses the redundancy inherent in ECG data through tokenization, potentially enhancing generalization and reducing overfitting. The observed zero-shot generalization to out-of-distribution datasets suggests the framework could be valuable in real-world clinical settings. Moreover, the efficient adaptation through LoRA tuning allows for quick adjustments to specific clinical tasks.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear and well-motivated approach:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges of integrating ECG data with LLMs and presents a sound and intuitive solution.
    * **Strong empirical results:** The experimental results demonstrate state-of-the-art performance on ECG question answering and diagnostic report generation tasks across multiple datasets. The ablation studies provide further evidence for the effectiveness of each component of the framework.
    * **Interpretability:** The visualizations of attention maps highlight the model's ability to focus on clinically meaningful regions of the ECG waveform, suggesting the framework is not just a black box but also has a degree of interpretability.
    * **Zero-shot generalization:** Good results in zero-shot out-of-distribution settings are promising.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on LLaMA:** The results heavily depend on LLaMA's architecture. It is unclear how easily it would transfer to other language models.
    * **Limited Scope:** The paper primarily focuses on ECG data and doesn't extensively explore the applicability of the framework to other types of physiological signals or medical data. While the core concepts are generalizable, further research is needed to validate this.
    * **Implementation Details:**  Some readers would value more detail on how the FSQ and tokenizer were implemented.

* **Potential Influence:** The DiagECG framework has the potential to influence future research in medical AI by promoting more structured and symbolic representations of physiological data for LLMs. It could also lead to the development of more accurate and interpretable diagnostic tools for cardiovascular diseases and other medical conditions. Future works may focus on extending the framework to other physiological signals, incorporating medical knowledge to improve interpretability, and developing real-time analysis systems.

* **Overall Assessment:** The paper presents a novel and technically sound approach to integrating ECG data with LLMs for diagnostic reasoning. The strong empirical results, combined with the framework's interpretability and potential for generalization, make it a significant contribution to the field. The paper's weaknesses are primarily related to the scope and limitations of the evaluation.

**Score: 7.5**
